- 2026-10-17 The review of the shared kit, Remember my gear and the expanders (fix round 1). **A restored list is a layer of its own.** The gear put back from the device is kept apart from what the visitor added by hand and from what a file gave (`restored` in `upgrade-input.js`): the next file that is read replaces it, as it replaces the file read before, so restoring and then reading the same dump gives the dump's counts and not twice them; what was added by hand stays; Remove and Clear take an item out of it. A restored item says "remembered" where a file's says where it is, and "added by hand" is only for what the visitor typed. **Two tabs.** A `storage` event that removes the key (the other tab turned the box off or erased the copy) sets this tab off too: the box unchecks, the writing stops, and the section says "Your remembered gear was erased from this device in another tab, so this page no longer remembers it." through `#announce`; a tab that has the box off ignores the event, and a write by another tab or the change of another key is not an erase. **A second kit link in the same tab.** `first.js` also listens for `hashchange`. A kit fragment that arrives while the page is open is taken out of the address the same way as at the start (into `history.state`, the fragment removed, `data-kit-fragment` set to what happened) and the page is reloaded, so it shows the new kit and not the first; the same link twice works, and a change of the fragment that is not a kit link (the skip link, an anchor) is left alone. **The order of the expanders.** The pump numbers an open expander when it first sees it open, not at the click, and sets the number back to 0 when it is closed: two expanders opened before the page was told of the first are worked out in the order they were opened. **The value of a shared kit is the kit's own.** A link carries the items and their places and nothing about the weapons, the haste or the buffs of the one who shared it, so none of the three is the page's to give. The setup is read from the hands: a two-hander in the main hand is "2h", a shield in the off hand "1h-shield", otherwise "dw", and a hand that holds something that is no weapon makes it "stick" (the setups are tried in that order, the first that lets both hands stand as they are is the kit's, and when none does the one that refuses the fewest is; what it refuses is left out). The haste is the model's own rule (`hastePercent` of `prune.js`: the best spell haste of the buff set plus the best worn haste of the items the value counts, up to the cap), not the page's, which moves with the tier and the weapons of its own answer. The panel says which: "Weapons: dual wield, read from the kit's hands, at 25% haste." (a stat stick has no haste to say). A kit shared from Equip now may therefore show another number than its sender saw (reference kit 0, an Ogre, balanced, own buffs, wiki evidence, is 16,267.22 as a shared kit, at 25% haste, and 16,691.04 as the sender's Equip now, at 40%, where a haste item the sender owns and does not wear counted); the line says what it was scored at. **Hands.** The legality check has the solver's hand rules and a reason of its own, "hands" ("a two-hander takes both hands"): a two-hander in the off hand, a two-hander in the main hand with an item in the off hand, and an item that the setup refuses in its hand are left out of the value and said in their rows (`allowedInHand` of `model.js` decides). **The buffs are the best there are.** The optional buffs are searched exactly, by a branch and bound: ranked by what each adds alone, decided one by one (in, then out) within the set's cardinality and its exclusions, and cut when the best a branch could reach, worked out by the evaluator itself with a buff of the best amounts of every component among those left, is no better than the best kit found. It is exact because the value never falls when a buff is added that takes nothing away (the weights are not negative, and haste is a maximum) and a buff that does take something away (Divine Purpose costs mana regeneration) is a choice, not a rule. The bound's buff is not in the bundle, and its id is above every id of the set and of the table (the keys of the table, the buffs that are always on, the optional ones, and those the exclusions and cliques name): the first version took the largest optional id plus one, which a buff that is always on can have, as the ids of one spell line are consecutive, and the bound's vector then stood in that buff's place and the evaluator refused the set (the kit had no value). The real results (`pal-mnk-ber`, level 50) have the same four sets as the fixture: own (1 optional buff), group (19 optional, at most 15, no exclusions), group with an enchanter (19, 15, none) and raid (32, 15, 8 exclusion cliques); the group set needs the C(19,4) = 3,876 subsets of size 15 if it is enumerated, and the search makes 57 calls at most on the fixture's kits and views (own 4, group with an enchanter 53); the raid set, which the page does not offer yet, 2,412. The worst case is the limit, 6,000 calls to the evaluator (a fifth of a second to a few seconds); a search that reaches it says so, the value is then the best found, and the panel says "at least" and "The best buffs were not searched out completely, so the kit may be worth more." **What the tests hold the value to.** `fixtures/site/kit-values.json` has the value of 6 kits (the 3 reference kits and 3 small ones: a haste hoop worn, a two-hander alone, a one-hander with a shield) under every role, buff set and evidence level the page offers, 66 values, each as Python's reference gives it (`engine_fixture.py`: `upgrade.equip_now` with the kit as the whole owned set, and `session_haste`); the page's value agrees with every one of them to the digit (the exact fraction), and with the worker's own choice of weapons and buffs (the real HiGHS, 58 rows) in `engine-kitvalue-worker.test.mjs`. The earlier tests of the value built the number they expected from the same view they were checking, and were replaced. **The evaluator is an engine file.** `engine/kitvalue.js` is a script of its own, `assets/engine/kitvalue-<HASH>.js`, listed in `engine-manifest.json` with its path, bytes, gzip size and SHA-256 and with budgets of its own (80 KB raw, 28 KB gzip), and the optimizer's client no longer exports it (it is 59,292 bytes again, as before the task). A kit link loads that file and the data it needs (the manifest, the bundle of the trio) and never the client, the worker or the solver. The page fetches the script, checks its size and its SHA-256 against the manifest, and only then imports it from the same address (so the browser is asked for it twice, once for the check and once for the import). The entry is optional in the page's reader (a manifest without one has a page that says the value cannot be shown) and required in the build, which refuses a manifest without it. **The panel is a chunk of its own.** `ui/shared-kit.js` and `input/kitdecode.js` (`decodeKit` and what it reads with; `kitcode.js` keeps the format, the tables and `encodeKit`, which Share and Remember use) are a chunk, `shared-kit-<HASH>.js`, that the section loads by a dynamic import when a kit link is opened and at no other time; a page with none never asks for it. The build refuses a chunk of the section that holds either file or imports at once an output that does (`KIT_INPUTS`, `check_page_script`), looking at esbuild's own account of the bundle. What the panel and the section share (Share's module, the tables) esbuild writes as `chunk-<HASH>.js`, which the section's chunk imports. When the panel's code cannot be had (a browser keeps a failed module fetch until the page is reloaded) the section's line and the page's status say "Couldn't load the shared kit. Reload the page to try again.", each with a Reload, and the section works on without the kit. **The budget of the code loaded on first use is one for each chunk.** One budget for all of it was spent by the first chunk that came, and the code of a shared kit would have been refused with the section's. A chunk is a script under `assets/` that no page loads by itself and the stylesheet esbuild writes beside it (the same name but for the suffix; styles with no script are a chunk of their own); each chunk has 100 KB raw and 40 KB gzip of its own (`LIMITS["lazy_chunk_js"]`, `LIMITS["lazy_chunk_js_gzip"]`), and all the chunks together 250 KB raw in all (`LIMITS["lazy_total_js"]`; no limit on their gzip in all). The build names the chunk that is over, and prints the number of chunks, the total and the largest chunk. The first load keeps its numbers (30 KB gzip, 100 KB raw). **Before and after**, on the fixture (the chunks and the engine files do not depend on the data): the page script 27,482 bytes raw and 9,549 gzipped, now 28,059 and 9,696 (first load with the boot script 27,857 and 9,789, now 28,434 raw of 100 KB and 9,936 gzipped of 30 KB; the `hashchange` listener, the words of a setup and the hook for a panel that cannot be had are in it); the stylesheet 18,102 and 4,204, unchanged; the chunk of the section 98,885 raw and 36,130 gzipped, now 85,066 and 31,176 (83.1% of 100 KB and 76.1% of 40 KB), with the chunk of the panel 7,937 and 3,683 and what the two share 8,271 and 3,799, 101,274 raw and 38,658 gzipped in all (39.6% of 250 KB); the optimizer's client 107,719 raw and 34,575 gzipped, now 59,292 and 18,808; the evaluator 65,381 and 22,468 (79.8% of 80 KB and 78.4% of 28 KB). A kit link asks for the section's chunk, the panel's, the shared one, the evaluator (twice, as said) and the bundle, and for none of the client, the worker or the solver; a page with no kit link asks for none of the panel's code or the evaluator. **A page that does not start.** On the real results, a page opened by the end-to-end helper `open()` stayed without its theme button for the whole wait about once in 13 runs, and the failure said only that the button was hidden. The wait is the 20 seconds it was and nothing is retried; the failure now lists what the page did while it loaded: the requests that failed, the ones that never finished, the answers with a status of 400 or more, the console errors and the uncaught exceptions (paths of addresses only, at most 20 of each kind), and a spec holds that with a page script that is never answered, refused, cut off and thrown.
- 2026-10-17 The weights of a best-kit request have a floor and a digit limit, and the page holds a best kit to the tier it asked for. **The bounds.** A slider position, a custom weight and the regen window of a best-kit request are zero, or at least 0.000001 in size (`WEIGHT_FLOOR`) and of at most 6 significant digits (`WEIGHT_DIGITS`, counted as the number prints: `Number(x.toPrecision(6)) === x`); a negative number is judged by its size. Zero is allowed, and so is null for the window (the role's own). **Why.** A weight is read as the decimal it prints as, so the digits of the weights set the size of the exact value of the answer, a fraction p/q that the page reads only up to 200 characters (`LIMITS.maxExactLength`) and calls a fault of the protocol when it is longer, dropping the worker. A custom weight of 5e-324 made it 657 characters and a regen window of 1e-100 made it 209, so a weight the bundle allows (anything from 0 to its largest, a window up to its longest) could cost the visitor the worker. With the bounds the longest exact value the protocol lets a request make (all fourteen custom weights, the five sliders and the window, each at the floor and with six digits) is some 40 characters, and the tests hold the worker's answer to it. **Where.** `isLegalWeightNumber` in `engine/protocol.js`, used by `checkWeights`, which is part of `validateRequest`: the page runs it before it asks the visitor anything, and the worker runs it again on every message (`validateToWorker`), so the two are one function and cannot drift; a request over the bounds is a bad request that names the rule, from the page and from the worker alike. **What is not here.** Which slider groups there are, the three positions, which weights may be set, the largest custom weight (1000) and the longest window (60 minutes) are the bundle's (`weight_groups`, `regen_ratios`), and `weightsOverride` holds the request to them in the worker, again as a bad request. **The tier.** The expectation that `client.js` builds for a request now has its `tier`, so a best kit whose items are all at another tier than the request's, with an exact value and a number that match, is a protocol fault and the worker is dropped; before, the page asked only that the items shared some tier.
- 2026-10-17 The PC release build (`scripts/release-build.py`, `trioforge.precompute.release`, `assemble`, `trioforge.reports.results_diff`): one trio is the unit of a pool of worker processes, each running `trioforge.precompute.run` with one process, because the robustness seals and the flip check of a run work in the process that made it and a pool of trios keeps every core busy through them (the seals and flip checks are about 24 CPU hours over the grid, and a pool inside one trio would have left them to one core); the heaviest trios start first, and `items.json` is written once by the parent before any worker starts, so that no two workers write it. A finished trio leaves `chunks/complete.json` (the fingerprint of its chunks, the data release, patch date and rules version of its headers, its scope and the digest of each result file); a trio under the same inputs whose files are all as left is skipped without being opened, and anything else runs again from its chunk files, as before (a chunk made from other inputs is made again; one made from the same inputs is kept, a changed scope included, since a chunk is one view). The snapshot id is **not** in the fingerprint of the chunks: that would make a second release of the same data redo the whole grid, the digest of the flags in it already redoes the chunks when the flags change, and chunk files never leave `build/`. The release is **staged** (hard links to the result files, never `chunks/`, any other file refused) so that every check and the scan read the very files that ship; the keyed scan labels a file by its place in the release (`pal-mnk-ber/wiki-own.json`), because the `build-path` rule would otherwise flag every file of a release that is staged under `build/`, and it also reads the text of the pointer and of the report before they are written; the asset is held to the manifest of what was scanned. The shipped bundles and item table are those the precompute writes (the public catalog, with the flags merged in memory at run time); the catalog with the flags merged (built from the same wiki fetch and client files into `build/catalog-with-seen`) is the reference they are held to: every bundle must be exactly what the exporter writes from it, its strict setups must be the shard headers', and the evidence level of every item must be the same in both. `catalog sha256` in the pointer is the **public** catalog's (the digest of a build made with the flags is never published). In `results_diff` a rule is in a pick only where the model reads it (an Iksar rule for an Iksar, a Monk rule for a trio with a Monk, the dual-wield factor for the dual-wield setup, the tier rules above +0; a rule with no scope is in every pick), a community entry explains a change only when it moves an item's evidence level, and a change nothing explains is a solver diff, a tie when the value is the same; a trial build (`--trios`) writes no pointer and is not compared with a release. Time of a full run, measured on the maintainer's computer with the real catalog and no seen flags, one process for each trio: war-rog-ber took 1,136 s for 84 chunks (13.5 s a chunk, 0.90 s a scenario, with the runner-ups, seals, flip checks and files), clr-nec-wiz (WIS and INT mana) 795 s for 30 chunks (26.5 s a chunk, 1.77 s a scenario), and the solves alone of PAL/MNK/BER took 19.95 s a chunk at +0, +4 and +10. The seals, flip checks and files are serial inside a run (about 230 s of the 326 s of war-rog-ber with 10 processes in one run), which is why the pool is over trios. The grid is 50,176 chunks (752,640 scenarios): 190 to 370 CPU hours with one process each and nothing else running, about 240 with the benchmark's mix of trio types (30% of the trios mix WIS and INT mana); 20 busy workers on 24 threads run about 1.3 times slower (reports/bench-2026-10-14.md: loaded against alone), so about 315 CPU hours: **about 16 hours of wall time at `--processes 20` (10 to 24)**, and under an hour more for the checks except the parity suite, the scan and the packing (timed on one trio: the bundle export 0.2 s, the browser's reader about a second, the keyed scan 2.6 s for 3 MB, the diff 0.8 s for 5,280 scenarios); the parity suite adds hours (see the parity sample below).
- 2026-10-17 The release build after review (`release.py`, `assemble.py`, `results_diff.py`): **what is committed names no item.** The full diff lists the items of every change and the evidence level of every item record that moved, which says which items the maintainer has seen (a key added to the private list shows as `wiki` to `strict`), so it is written to `build/reports/results-diff-<tag>.json` (away from the asset, so that an upload of `build/release/<tag>.*` cannot publish it) and never to `reports/`; only the Markdown goes to git (counts by kind and cause, the rules and inputs that changed, solver diffs, the 20 largest changes of F with their causes and no item, and the community entries, which are public in `rules/community-seen.json` and which a retraction must list). **No place of this computer is published.** The note on a previous release whose asset is not in the build folder names the tag only and is said as a warning at the start, and before the keyed scan a keyless check reads every published text for a drive letter, a share, a home or system folder or a file URL, whatever the build folder is called (the scan's rule against build paths sees only a folder named `build`). **The code hash of a release covers the code that writes the files.** It is the SHA-256 of the hash of the chunk code and the hash of `assemble.WRITER_FILES` (the runner, the shards, the seals, the bundle and item table, the strict-JSON writer and the scope); `chunks/complete.json` holds the writer hash, a finished trio under another writer is written again from its chunks (nothing is solved), and a test follows what those files import. A change to the code that decides a solve is still the fingerprint of the chunks. **Rules.** A change to a rule the solver never reads (it uses a constant that mirrors it, or only the export and the site read it: `results_diff.UNREAD`, 26 of 53, kept true by a test that follows the string constants of the hashed code and by a real solve) explains no pick, so it hides no solver diff; a rule's value and levels count and its status does not (`release.json` keeps no status). **Seals and bundles** are diffed: a changed seal is a record with the places that differ and a bundle is named with the SHA-256 of its file in each release, never its content; both are explained by the same causes, and a change nothing explains is flagged as a solver diff. **A trial** writes under `build/trial/` (its asset and its two reports, both headed TRIAL), never to `reports/` or `releases/`, and uses up no tag; a release is the whole grid. **The parity sample** always includes the defaults of every trio, its offset is drawn from the snapshot id of the release, its stride is at most the number of chunks of the smallest trio (so every trio has chunks in it; the default is that largest stride), and `release.json` records it as `parity_sample`. The defaults of every trio are the views of about two or three chunks a trio, some 1,400 chunks of 15 races for the grid, on top of the sample (one real chunk, strict at +5 of PAL/MNK/BER, took 2.9 s in the engine; the suite on the whole grid takes hours and was not timed in full). **The keyed scan** refuses a denylist with no key-check line (any key would pass). **One snapshot of the seen flags** is copied into `build/` at the start and read by every worker and check, so flags made again during the hours cannot mix two states. **A killed build** leaves a staging folder, a scratch folder and a temporary asset under the tag in `build/release/`, which the next build of the tag removes by their exact names.
- 2026-10-17 The release build after the second review (`release.py`, `grid.py`, `assemble.py`, `results_diff.py`): **the code is hashed once.** A worker runs the code it imported, and a module it imports later is read from the disk as it is then, so hashing the files in each worker (as `grid.load_inputs` and the trio marker did) would stamp chunks with the hash of files changed after the code that made them was loaded (a merge into the checkout in the middle of 16 hours). The parent hashes the code of the chunks and the code that writes the files once, before the pool; they travel in `TrioSpec` (`chunk_code`, `writer_code`) to the workers, which pass the first to `grid.load_inputs(code_hash=...)` and `precompute.run(code_hash=...)` and write the second in `complete.json`, and the pruning check does the same; `release.json` holds `assemble.combine_code_hashes` of those two values. After the precompute, and again just before the asset is packed, the parent hashes the files again and refuses the release if either hash changed (nothing is packed, no pointer is written, the chunks stay under the hashes of the start). The command line of the precompute keeps its behaviour (it hashes the files itself). The build is run from a worktree of its own at the commit to release. **Smaller:** the keyless path check also refuses `file:` with one slash or more and a share written with forward slashes (`//host/share/`, with no ':' or word character before the slashes, so that web addresses pass: the real catalog has no finding); the chunks of a trio are the views of its shards, each counted once, as the parity suite numbers them (a bundle of another level adds none and cannot lift the stride limit); the scratch folder of the bundle check must be a `*.bundles` folder beside the release folder, because what is at its name is removed; a changed rules version is a rule cause of a bundle change (the bundle's inputs are one hash over the whole rules file, a status or a note included), so a change of the rules file outside the value or levels of any rule is no solver diff; and the full diff is `build/reports/results-diff-<tag>.json`, so that nothing in `build/release/` but the asset is named for the tag.
- 2026-10-17 The release build after the third review (`release.py`, `results_diff.py`, `release-build.py`): **the checkout and the inputs do not change during the build.** The code hash was one part of that; `build_release` now also refuses a root that is not a git worktree (`release.Git`) and records, once, at the start, `release.Start`: the two code hashes, `HEAD`, a digest of `git status --porcelain=v1 --untracked-files=all`, a digest of `git diff HEAD --binary`, the parsed rules (and the SHA-256 of their file) and the SHA-256 of the public catalog; `release.json` is written from those values, and `check_code_unchanged` compares all of it again after the precompute and just before the pack, in the same words. A pool worker runs `_init_worker` when it starts: it imports every module of the two hashed lists, hashes the files, and refuses its trios if the hashes are not the parent's, so a worker respawned after a change cannot make a chunk from changed code under the hashes of the start (`precompute_grid(check_workers=False)` leaves it out for a test that gives hashes no disk has). **A killed run leaves nothing that looks publishable.** At the start of a build of a tag with no pointer the build removes, by exact name, its asset, the full diff of the first round (`build/release/<tag>.results-diff.json`) and an untracked `reports/results-diff-<tag>.md`, and refuses a tracked one with its reason (the old refusal for any report on disk was a trap after a kill); packing, checking the asset and writing the pointer are one transaction that removes the asset on any error; a failure of `say` (a closed pipe) is no failure of the build, and the command exits 0 with a warning on standard error when the pipe closes after the outputs are written. **Private files.** The seen copy, the full diff, a trial's files and the temporary file of each are checked before they are written: the path is resolved, a link or junction on the way inside its work tree is refused, and `git check-ignore` is asked about the path itself (the catalog's check asks about four file names, which a narrow `.gitignore` can satisfy). **One row for an item.** `check_item_rows` refuses a release in which an item has a row in one shard or trio that differs from its row in another, and the diff compares the rows of every shard instead of the first one's. **The results diff says why more exactly.** A move of an item's evidence level explains a scenario only when it changes the item's membership of the pool the scenario was made from (strict pool: level strict; wiki pool: strict or wiki), a change of a solver field explains every scope, and a community entry explains a move the same way. The runner-ups are walked over the union of the views of both releases, and the evidence level of a view is compared with its setup and swaps. A default is compared whole (race, runner-up, setup, value, margin, kit, the view with its evidence and buff set, flip notes), and one that a release lacks is added or removed. The gain is compared (the naked value, F less the gain, beyond two decimals of rounding). A bundle that differs in its `inputs` field alone is explained by any input change; one that differs elsewhere only by the code, the rules or the patch, so a change of an item's source text is a solver diff if a bundle moved for it. **Left as it is, by decision:** the staged files are not guarded against a hostile writer, `os.path.isjunction` is the check for links on Python 3.12, there is no general rule for POSIX paths in the keyless check, and an alternative of the same value is a tie, not a solver diff.
- 2026-10-17 Item pages: `/item/<key>/`. **What gets one.** Every wearable (it lists a slot of a place of a kit: `build.WEARABLE_SLOTS`, which a test holds equal to the precompute's) whose evidence level in the item table of the results is strict or wiki (`build.item_page_keys`). The level is the table's own, the one the kits were solved with, never a private flag, so a page and the kits of a build agree. A disputed or a hidden item has no page, nothing links to it, and a name is plain text where its item has no page (`view.kit_rows`, `build.revision_rows`). A page credits its wiki page, so an item that has a wiki page and a page of its own but no entry in the sources manifest stops the build; an item with no wiki page (its record has no page id: an `r` key, `view.has_wiki_page`) has nothing to credit, needs no entry, and its page has no credit section. A key that two records of the item table share is refused whatever their levels, since the page of a key is built from the record a lookup keeps. The keys feed `site_files(item_pages=...)`, which counts the files of a site exactly: 47 pages on the fixture (48 records, one of them hidden). The number of pages of the real results is written nowhere, in this file or any other or in a commit message: the private seen flags decide it (an item a sighting rescued from hidden has a page), and `test_site_counts.py` works such figures out from the real results at run time and refuses them. **What a page says.** The facts (slots, classes, races, deity, Lore, weight); the stats of every tier from +0 to +10 as `at_tier` works them out from the catalog item rebuilt from the record, figure for figure and with nothing between two tiers estimated (the tables of the 47 pages of the fixture and of twenty real items, weapons among them, are held to `at_tier`, and a build of the real results was read back the same way, page by page, with no difference); for a weapon its damage and delay by tier with the seal of `tier.weapon_damage_pct`; the sources the table holds, with the places that did not fit counted; the evidence, with the seal and the words of the page's own level (strict or wiki), and the notes of the table that are about the item (not-for-bis, not-for-default-kits, wiki-marked-revamped), each in words with its seal; the trios whose default answer wears the item (in the order of their slugs, 20 at most, then "and N more", and "none" said plainly); and the credit to the wiki page and its revision (none for an item that has no wiki page). **Reasons are not shown.** The item table keeps the reason codes of the public catalog on an item that a seen flag made strict, so a reason shown would tell which items a sighting rescued, and a page would show the words of a level that is not its own. A page words its evidence by its level only, and the reasons of the evidence (`view.EVIDENCE_CODES`: the hidden and the disputed ones, and the strict ones, which the level says already) are never shown; the notes about the item (`view.ITEM_NOTES`) are shown, in words. A code that is in neither list stops the build with the item named (a test holds the two lists to the codes of `data/evidence.py`, so a new code has to be worded or set aside before a page can meet it), where a code shown raw would have gone onto the page. The words of the wiki level say that the wiki lists a source the site counts as live, since that source can be a drop, a quest, a vendor, a recipe or foraging, and a page of an item with no source says that the item table lists none. **The table scrolls.** Eleven tiers of figures do not fit a 360 px window, and a card for each stat would repeat the label of every tier eleven times, so the table scrolls sideways inside its own region (it has a tab stop, the arrow keys scroll it, and the names of the stats stay in view) and the page itself never does. The region is named by the caption of its table, so that it does not share the name of its section (two landmarks of one name fail axe), and the end-to-end run of the item pages fails on moderate findings too. **Budgets.** A page has 16 KB raw and 4 KB gzip (`LIMITS["item_html"]` and `LIMITS["item_html_gzip"]`); `check_budgets` refuses a page over either, naming it, and a page that loads anything but the stylesheet, the boot script and the page script or has a script of its own. On the real results the largest page is 11,011 B raw and the heaviest gzipped 3,019 B (the middle page is 8,151 B raw); with twenty trios named in Best for, which is the most a page names, the largest reach 12,650 B raw and 3,264 B gzipped (12.4 and 3.2 KB of 1,024 B). The raw limit was 12 KB at first and left no room for those twenty links, so it is 16 KB; the gzip limit stays at 4 KB. **Links.** The 22 names of Wear this link to their pages, and the page code does the same for the kits it paints when a switch changes (`data-items="/item/"`, which the build writes on every trio page, since every item a trio page can show has a page: kits hold strict and wiki wearables only, and a trio page that could show an item with no page stops the build, naming the trio and the item, where it would lose every link without a word; a key that is not an item key is never linked: `data.KEY_PATTERN` is the one pattern of an item key, which the reader of the results, the list of the pages and the page code (`ITEM_KEY` of `trio.js`) all use, and a test holds the three to each other, the 101-character key included). The sources page lists the items that the trio pages show, as before, each title a link to its page, and says that each item page carries its own credit (the wiki page, its revision, the licence and the changes made): listing every item that has a page made the page many times larger, and CC BY-SA asks for no such list when each page credits its own source. Two items of one name get titles that differ by their keys. The names in the runner-ups, the weapons block, the robust core and the Upgrade section do not link: the trio page has 2,032 B of its 61,440 B left on the real results, and the names of its baked runner-ups (22 places, up to 4 names in each, about 28 bytes a link) would cost up to 2.4 KB. The sitemap is not changed. **Before and after**, on the real results: the HTML of the trio page 58,772 B, now 59,408 (22 links and `data-items`); the page script 28,059 B raw and 9,695 gzipped, now 28,444 and 9,862 (the links the code paints); the stylesheet 18,102 and 4,222, now 18,694 and 4,321 (the table of tiers); the first load of the trio page 22.3 KB gzipped, now 22.7 of its 60; the sources page about 37 KB, now 40,873 B raw and 7,858 gzipped (the link on each title).
- 2026-10-17 Weights in every mode, the weapon tier rate and a guard for the pruning (the engine side of the reweighting). **Weights.** Equip now, the farm path, the best sets and Every slot take the optional `weights` of the best kit, `{multipliers?, custom?, regenT?}`: the same allowlist, the same shape and the same numeric rule (zero, or at least 0.000001 in size with at most 6 significant digits), read in one place of `protocol.js` for all five modes, in the page and in the worker alike. The bundle's bounds are `weightsOverride`'s, and a request over them is a bad request from the worker. One function puts the weights on a profile, `viewProfile` of `engine/bestkit.js`, which the best kit alone used and the session of `scenario.js` uses now: they are composed on the role's own only when they are not the role's own (every slider at x1, no custom weight and no window change nothing and share the session of a request with none), a slider group the bundle lacks is refused at x1 as at x2, and weight rules the engine cannot use are a fault of the data (`engine-load`, the bundle dropped), found only by a request that has weights. A session is kept by its weights as well (`weightsKey`: one key for the tables in any order, a slider at x1 or a window of null, another for any other weights), so a request is worked out for its own weights and no other. The assumed haste is still the model's rule (`session_haste`: the best spell haste on offer and the best worn haste of the owned items and the candidates): the weights do not move it. A stop, a cancel and a request that replaces another keep the rules they had (`client.js` is unchanged): a stop ends a weighted path `stopped` and keeps the worker, a cancel drops the worker, and the next request is answered for its own weights. The candidates do not depend on the weights, so the last list made is kept across them. **The fixtures.** `fixtures/site/weighted.json` has each Equip now scenario of the site fixture with weights drawn from a fixed seed (`"trioforge-weights:<scenario>:<draw>"`, a function of the text alone; a draw whose answers depend on the tie stage, which the browser has none of, is skipped) and a tier mode in turn (ready4, arrives with D 2, fixed +6): Python's Equip now, a farm path of three steps with its start at the session's haste, the best 2 and 3 sets and Every slot from the kit of Equip now, for a profile whose weights are `roles.weights_override`'s (`upgrade.py`; 6 cases, 18 steps of which 15 are new items, 2 copies and 1 an upgrade, 12 sets that all acquire something, 307 swaps; the weights move Equip now's value in every case, its kit in 5 and its buffs in 1). The worker equals them exactly: the value as a reduced fraction, the kit place for place, the buffs, the haste, every step (item, how it is got, source tier, value, gain, kit, buffs), what each set acquires and every swap. They are the site fixture's, made by `pipeline/tests/engine_fixture.py` from the invented catalog, because the worker is held to them through the bundle and the item table it loads, which the families of `export/fixtures.py` do not hold it to; those are unchanged (their model and evaluator cases already carry composed weights, and `path.js` reads the weights only through the profile of its session). **The weapon tier rate.** The best kit takes `options: {weaponTierRate: 5 | 10}`, the percent of damage a weapon grows a tier (the rule `tier.weapon_damage_pct`, 5, is the default; `at_tier(weapon_pct=)` is the reference). `options` is an allowlist in `protocol.js` (the key `weaponTierRate`, the values 5 and 10 as numbers, a plain object; a best kit has it and no other mode) and the worker holds the engine to it again. The items of the pool are made with the rate (`atTier`'s `weaponPct`), so the pruning, which compares the damage the items carry, and the evaluator, which values the kit's own items, use it without being told; nothing else reads the rate, and at +0 no weapon has grown, so the two rates are one answer. `fixtures/site/weapon-rate.json` has 7 views at 5, at 10 and with no rate (equal to 5): the rate moves the value in 6 of them and the kit in 4, and the worker equals Python's at each. **A guard for the pruning.** `bestKit` prunes with the race's own profile and the precompute with the first race of the chunk that wears the same race-only items; they are one pruning because `prune` reads nothing that differs between races but the race (the 2,880 race-views of the 9a review found no difference). `tests-js/best-kit-prune-guard.test.mjs` makes that permanent over the views of the results (the 600 race-views of the invented catalog's 40 chunks, every race of the bundle; `TRIOFORGE_RESULTS` and `TRIOFORGE_SHARD_SAMPLE` as in the parity test) and over seeded pools of one to four race-only items, in two ways: the kept items are the precompute's rule's, and `prune` is run with a profile that records what is read of it and of its compiled form (`profileOf` of the evaluator is wrapped in the test; the compile, which reads the whole profile once, is not counted), which must be none of the fields that differ between the races of the bundle (worked out from the bundle: `naked` and `iksar_ac`, compiled `naked` and `iksarAc`) but the race. A pruning that merely read the naked attributes fails it on any view, whether or not it changed a kept item; one whose result depended on the Iksar armor class fails the comparison as well (both were tried on `prune.js` and reverted). `bestkit.js` gained `viewPool` (the profile, the pool and the pruned pool of a view, which `bestKit` calls) so that the test holds the code the worker runs.
- 2026-10-18 Fix round of the weights in every mode. **Every request is checked against the bundle first.** The key of a session leaves out what changes nothing (a slider at x1, an empty table, a window of null), and the kept session was returned before `viewProfile` looked at the weights, so a slider group the bundle does not have, set to x1, was refused on a fresh worker and answered after a request with none, and the four Upgrade modes behaved unlike the best kit. `sessionFor` of `scenario.js` now calls `viewProfile` for every request, and uses the key only to say which session to reuse; a refusal makes no session and closes none (it used to close the kept one before it refused). **A wrong choice and the bundle's own fault.** `assembleProfile` says that a race, a role, a buff set, an evidence level or a deity the bundle does not have is the asker's with a `ChoiceError` (a `BundleError`, so every `instanceof BundleError` still holds); `viewProfile` turns that, and only that, into a `BestKitError`, a bad request in every mode with the bundle kept, and any other error of the profile (the bundle's rules of weights, a profile it cannot give for choices it has) is the bundle's own: engine-load, the bundle dropped. Before, every `BundleError` of `assembleProfile` was taken for a wrong choice, so a fault of the bundle there was a bad request; no wrong choice was ever taken for a fault of the bundle, as the review feared (`engine-view-choices.test.mjs` holds both, in the four Upgrade modes and the best kit). **The guard sees every way a field is read.** The recording of `best-kit-prune-guard.test.mjs` noted the compiled profile through `get` only, and the raw profile through `get`, its descriptor and `in`; one `noting` proxy now serves both, so a pruning that reads `naked` through `Object.getOwnPropertyDescriptor`, `in` or `Object.keys` is found by the recording and not only by the grep of its source (tried on `prune.js` and reverted).
- 2026-10-18 The release build after the cross-family review of the build (`release.py`, `release-build.py`): **a decision to delete fails closed.** `Git.tracked` answers with git's own word only (status 0 for a file that is tracked, status 1 for one that is not); a git that does not start, does not answer in time or stops on a damaged index, and a path outside the work tree, raise `ReleaseRefused`, so `clear_unfinished` cannot take a failure of git for "not tracked" and remove a report it promises to refuse. The temporary files of a cut-short write of the pointer or of the report get the check the report has, and every decision of `clear_unfinished` comes before its first removal, so a refusal leaves everything as it was found. **Private outputs and the build folder.** The files of `build/catalog-with-seen/` (the four names the catalog builder writes, and the temporary name of each) are private outputs with the per-path check, which `check_seen_output` (four names) does not give; `check_build_folder` asks git about a path inside the build folder (`BUILD_PROBE`) before the first private file is written, so a folder that `.gitignore` covers in part is refused at the start and not by the state check after the hours, and a folder outside the work tree passes. **The command's output.** A missing standard output (`sys.stdout` is None, as under pythonw) counts as closed and the final flush is guarded, so a good build exits 0; a line is written with `errors="replace"`, so an encoding that cannot write a character costs the character and no longer silences the lines after it, and only a pipe or file that is really closed stops the printing. **Left as it is, by decision:** the probe asks about one path in the build folder, so a rule that ignores its direct children and un-ignores a subfolder (`build/*` and `!build/release/`) is not found by it; every record of the real item table has a wiki page id today, and an item with no wiki page that a trio page shows is still refused by the check of the sources manifest for the sources page (the list of the wiki revisions has nothing to say of it).
- 2026-10-17 The sliders and the buff choices of the trio page. **What a shard answers.** The shards answer the own buffs and the common group buffs with every slider at x1, and nothing else (`view.exact` in `trio.js`). The full raid, the Enchanter in the group (a box under the group choice, on only while the group is chosen; with it the buff set is `group+enchanter`) and any slider at x0.5 or x2 are worked out by the optimizer in the visitor's browser, as a best-kit request for the view the page shows: the race, role, tier and weapons shown, the evidence, the buff set, and `weights.multipliers` for the sliders that are not at x1 (no `weights` for the role's own). Until the answer comes the page shows the shard of the own buffs for the own buffs and the shard of the common group buffs for the other three, and the weapons are the role's best under that shard or the visitor's choice: the optimizer is not asked which setup is best under the weights. **One request at a time, the latest winning.** `runScenario(request, {replaceWaiting: true})` (`client.js`): a best-kit request takes the place of the best-kit requests that have not reached the worker (each is rejected as `cancelled`: the ones that wait, and the one the client has begun for that still waits for the visitor's answer to the question or for the worker to start, so that a yes does not solve what was thrown away), and the one the worker has finishes; only requests of the best kit are replaced, and only by one. A request that is refused as busy replaces nothing (the queue is looked at as it would be without the ones it would replace). The page sends a request 300 ms after the last change, and only the latest settings may draw, say or complain of anything: the answer or the fault of a request for settings that are no longer shown is dropped. No answer is kept: a slider moved and back is the shard's kit and no request, and any other return asks again. **The answer area.** "Working out the kit for these settings…" with Stop (not while the client's question is open: there is nothing to work on yet), then "Solved for your settings" with the kit value, the exact value F and a button back. A fault, a Stop or a no to the question leaves the shard's kit and says that it is the precomputed answer for the nearest settings and not the visitor's, with Try again (Load the optimizer after a no). Stop takes back the request of the sliders alone (below); a solve cannot be interrupted from outside, so a request the worker has stops the worker, which starts again from the module the client kept, with no download. While the visitor's answer to the question is awaited the area says "The optimizer is waiting for your answer to the question below", with a button that moves the focus to the question, which is there once the question is in the page (the client is the page's one, so its question is mounted where its first caller put it: in the Upgrade section when it came first, and far from the sliders), and the question comes after the line. The client has no event for the answer, and the sliders' request may have nothing to report for a while after it (it can wait behind the request the question was asked for), so the line looks at the state of the question every 250 ms while it stands, and once it is answered says that the work is on, with Stop. **One client, two askers.** The sliders and the Upgrade section have requests on the same client, so neither calls `cancel()`, which ends all of them (it stays the page's: the Cancel of the loading panel, the end of the client). `runScenario(request, {signal, onSent})` takes one request back: a request that waits leaves the queue and a request the worker has stops the worker, and no other request is touched; `onSent` says when the worker has the request. Each side keeps a controller for each request: Stop takes the sliders' request back; a change of the view, or the page's going back to a precomputed answer, takes it back if the worker has not got it (a queued best kit nobody wants is not solved, up to 14 s on a phone, with the Upgrade section's requests behind it) and leaves a running one to end; the Upgrade section stops its path as before when the worker has it, and takes back a path that waits and the request of an expander. A request that comes back `cancelled` which its side did not cause, with the optimizer agreed to, is asked for again, once; the Cancel button of the loading panel marks its error `byVisitor`, and nothing is asked again for that. Each attempt at an answer has a number and goes on after a wait only if it is the latest and Stop was not pressed, so that Stop and Try again are not undone by the optimizer's code or the item list arriving. An answer that comes while the page loads another view is not drawn or said: `drawSolved` says no, the chunk keeps the answer, and `release()` draws it if the page stays where it was (`fail()` calls it); the next view's own `settle` drops it. The button back puts every slider at x1 and the raid and the Enchanter's group at the common group buffs (the own buffs stay): the nearest settings that a shard answers. **What the answer does not have.** A kit, its value, its F and the haste it was scored at; not the totals of the kit, its gain over no gear, the other weapon setups, a runner-up or a seal of the robust core. Why this kit and Weapons are out of sight while an answer is shown (the class `solved` of the page), and the runner-ups and the robust core say that they are for the shards' settings (they are held to every slider at x1 now). The names, sources and seals of the 22 items are read from the item table of the site (path, size and SHA-256 from the manifest; the worker fetched the same file and the browser has it), in the Upgrade section's words for a source, and only the fields a row shows are kept. **The address.** The query has the buff set (`buffs=own`, `group`, `enchanter`, `raid`: the Enchanter's group is a word with no plus sign for a browser to read as a space, and the older `group+enchanter`, escaped or typed with a raw plus sign, is read too) and `w`, a character (h, 1, 2) for each slider group of the bundle in the bundle's order, left out when every slider is at x1. A `w` of another length or with another character, or given twice, is ignored, said once in #status (with a notice about the weapons, if there is one), and left out of the address as it is written again; nothing else of the weights is in the address, and nothing the visitor owns. A page opened with settings that no shard answers asks the question and solves, or, after a no, shows the shard's kit and offers the optimizer. The address names the settings the visitor asked for (a reload works them out again); the link of the footer that reports a problem names the answer the table shows, so a report can be looked at as it was seen: the shard of the nearest settings until the answer of the optimizer is drawn, and the settings then. **The Upgrade section.** Its requests carry the weights of the view (every mode takes them since 9c1) and the buff set, the raid and the Enchanter's group included: its answers are for the sliders now, and the section has no line about the weights. The runner-ups and the robust core are for the role's own weights as well as for the default evidence, buffs and weapons, and their notes say so. **The chunk.** The sliders, the request, the answer area and the item list are `ui/weights.js`, which no page loads by itself: the page script loads it the first time the Weights assumption is opened, or the raid or the Enchanter is used, or the address asks for either (`check_weights_chunk` refuses a page script that holds it). When the code comes at a later use after it could not be had, the failure and its Reload button go, and the view the page shows is worked out then. The page carries 608 bytes for it: the Full raid choice 168, the Enchanter box 159, the Weights assumption with nothing in it 240 and the names of the bundle's slider groups (`data-groups`) 41. The chunk works on the page's own object (`initWeights(app, words)`), so the glue that only it calls (the change of the sliders, the button back, the settings it works out for, what it is given) is its code and not the page script's. **The size of the trio page.** The words that every row of a column said again are said once: the labels a narrow window puts before a fact (`data-label` of five columns) are the stylesheet's (`td:nth-child(n)::before`; the label of the runner-ups' cells was not used by any rule), and the rules the figures of a column follow are on the header of the column (`data-rules` of the Tier and Arrives-at headers) and on the body of the runner-ups' table; `test_site_build.py` refuses a table of the page in which a label or a rule is said in half of its rows, and reads the rules of a figure from its column's header. The full grid has longer names and kits, and the page has the room. **Before and after**, on the real results (main before this task, then this task's first round, now): the HTML of the trio page 59,408 B, 60,016 and 53,932 of its 61,440 (7,508 B of margin); the page script 28,444 B raw and 9,862 gzipped, 32,767 and 11,062, now 32,377 and 11,060 (the first load with the boot script 28,819 and 10,102, 33,142 and 11,302, now 32,752 and 11,300, of 100 KB and 30 KB); the stylesheet 18,694 and 4,321, 19,603 and 4,503, now 19,514 and 4,473; the first load of the trio page 22.7 KB gzipped, now 24.0 of its 60; the code loaded on first use 101,322 B raw and 38,673 gzipped in three chunks (the section's 85,114 and 31,191, the kit panel's 7,937 and 3,683, what they share 8,271 and 3,799), 110,500 and 43,799 in six, now 112,948 and 44,715: the section's 74,249 and 27,698 (the largest, of 100 KB and 40 KB), the kit panel's 8,211 and 3,792, the sliders' 9,959 and 4,367, and three that they share, 11,981 and 4,802, 6,038 and 2,881, 2,510 and 1,175 (of the 250 KB in all). A visitor who uses the sliders loads the chunk and the two it imports, 24,450 B raw and 10,344 gzipped.
- 2026-10-18 The home picker, the canonical address of a trio and the 404 page. **The address.** The page of a trio is `/trio/<x>-<y>-<z>/` with the classes in the order of `classes.CLASS_CODES` (pal-mnk-ber, never ber-pal-mnk): the one address the build writes, and the one that its `<link rel="canonical">`, the sitemap, the list on the home page and the list in the 404 page all name (`data.parse_slug` already refuses a folder of results in another order, and a test holds the lists to each other on a build of five trios). **Other orders are moved by the 404 page, not by redirects.** Six orders of each of the 560 trios are 2,800 addresses besides the right ones, and Cloudflare Pages takes 2,000 static redirects, so `404.html` (a real 404, titled "Page not found · TrioForge", which names no address because the same page is sent for all of them) has a script of its own that moves the visitor to the page of the trio. **Accepted limitation:** a crawler that is given a link in another order is given the 404 page and does not run the script, so a non-canonical link shared on Discord previews as "Not found"; only the canonical link previews as the trio. **The steps of the script** (`static/canonical.js`, tested in `tests-js/canonical.test.mjs` with every order of five trios, a long table of hostile addresses and a seeded run of 30,000 made-up ones), in the order they are taken: the path is cut at its slashes and each piece decoded once (a piece that cannot be decoded is refused; an encoded slash can never stand for a separator, since the cutting is done first, and what is still encoded after one decoding is refused, which is what a double-encoded address is); it is normalised (the slashes at both ends go, and every piece must be letters and hyphens only, checked before the case is folded, because the Kelvin sign folds into a k; that refuses dot-segments, backslashes, colons, spaces, control characters and look-alikes of every kind); the trio is looked up in the list of the build (`data-trios` on `<main>`: the codes in alphabetical order give the slug in the order of the classes, a trio that two entries spell differently is not guessed between, and a trio the list does not have is refused); only then is `location.replace` called, with `/trio/<slug>/` made from the list's own spelling, followed by `location.search` and `location.hash` exactly as the browser gave them (they are appended and never decoded, encoded or looked at). `isSafeTarget` checks that address once more (it starts with `/trio/`, ends in a slash and names a trio of the list), and an address that is the page's own is not moved to (a 404 on the right address would otherwise load itself for ever). The move replaces the entry of the 404 page in the history, and a visitor who is being moved is told so ("Taking you to the right page") in place of "Page not found". **The fragment of a kit link arrives untouched.** The 404 page loads `notfound-<HASH>.js` in place of the page script, a bundle of its own made of `notfound.js`, `canonical.js` and `theme.js` and nothing else: `check_notfound_script` stops the build for any other file in it, above all `first.js`, which takes the `#k=` payload out of the address and would have cleared the fragment before the page of the trio could (the end-to-end test records the address each document is given at its start: the second is the typed one's query and fragment byte for byte, and the page then shows the shared kit). **No analytics beacon.** The 404 page is the page without the guard for a kit link, so it may carry no analytics beacon, and a build that adds one later cannot put one there by accident: the frame's page script is a block (`page_script`) that the 404 template replaces whole, so a beacon placed in that block does not reach it, and `check_budgets` refuses a 404 page that carries one anywhere (the host `cloudflareinsights`, `beacon.min.js` or `data-cf-beacon`), that has a script other than the boot script and its own, or that loads any other file. **The 404 page's own policy.** The page gives a policy of its own, `<meta http-equiv="Content-Security-Policy" content="script-src 'self'; connect-src 'none'">` (`NOTFOUND_POLICY`), in its head before anything it loads (the frame has a `head_policy` block for it, empty on every other page): a page's own policy adds to the header's, so nothing on the page can load a script of another site or send anything, not even a beacon that Cloudflare's Web Analytics toggle (which the launch checklist keeps off) put into it, or one that the header of an analytics build would let come. The end-to-end test gives the header the hosts of the beacon and puts the tag at the end of a page: the home page runs it, and the 404 page does not. `check_budgets` requires the element, once, with exactly that content, before the first stylesheet or script, and the build requires all three of its files in the script of the page (`check_notfound_script`: a script that had dropped its import of `canonical.js` would have shipped a 404 page that moves no one). The theme button is bound first, so that it works while a visitor waits for the page they are being moved to. **A visitor who is lost.** An address of an item that has no page, or of a trio that leads nowhere, gets a hint with a link to `/#search`, which puts the cursor in the search box of the home page; the hint is made by the script from words of its own, and the address is never put into the page. **The home page.** The picker is a section that stays hidden until the script runs (with its heading, so a visitor without the script is not shown a heading with nothing under it), with a search box and the sixteen chips. The search (`readQuery` in `static/picker.js`) reads letters only and takes any other character as a separator: a class is named by its code or its full name, in any case, in any order, and a name of two words ("shadow knight") as two words or one; a class counts once, and the words that only join the classes (and, or, plus, the, trio: the last for an address that was pasted) are passed over; when more than three are named the first three are used and the page says so; a word that names no class is named in the status (three at most, twelve letters each, never the whole of what was typed), except the last word when it begins the name of a class and is still being typed, and the last two when the first is the first word of a name of two words and the second begins its second ("shadow k"); a word that begins a class after three full ones says that the first three are used, as a fourth class does. The chips and the box say one thing: what is typed sets the chips, a chip that is chosen is written into the box, and Enter opens the page when the status offers one. A link to a page of the site that is pasted into the box is not read for classes (it was read as the words https, trioforge and pages, or, with the path, as a trio with the query and the shared kit lost): when it is the page of a trio of the build, in any order of the classes, the link that is offered is `trioAddress` of it, with the query and the fragment it came with, and `isSafeTarget` checks it once more; any other address, of this site or of another, is told to be opened in the address bar. The status line is a live region, which a screen reader says again whenever it is replaced, so it is replaced only when its words or its link change. The box takes at most 2,500 characters (`MAX_QUERY_LENGTH` of `static/picker.js`, which is the `maxlength` of the box; a test holds the two equal and above the longest link to a shared kit, about 2,200 characters) and the picker reads no more than that, so that a paste of megabytes costs a key no more than a few thousand characters do; a limit of 200 characters would have cut the kit of every link that is pasted. A trio that is not built says "X: coming soon. There is no page for this trio yet." and offers no link. The picker shows no race, so there is nothing for it to put in the order of the creation-legal races first, which the race switch of the trio page does. **The list.** The page of every built trio is a link in the home page itself, open up to 12 trios and folded into an expander beyond that (a list of all 560 is most of the page and is no use open), and in the page for a visitor without the script and for a search engine; the page says "Every other trio is coming soon." until all 560 are built and nothing after (the heading is "Trios" and the fold "List all 560 trios": no word that the site is unfinished). The 404 page names the trios it can open up to 8 and none beyond. **Budgets.** `check_budgets` holds both pages to limits of their own, the home page 80 KB raw and 12 KB gzip and the 404 page 16 KB raw and 5 KB gzip, to the budgets of a first load (60 KB gzip, with the 30 KB gzip and 100 KB raw of the script), and to loading the stylesheet, the boot script and one script and nothing else. Before and after, on the fixture: the page script 28,444 B raw and 9,863 B gzipped, now 32,681 and 11,550 (the search, the pasted link, the status line); the home page 7,559 B and 2,378 B gzipped, now 7,980 and 2,541; the 404 page 5,133 B and 1,846 B gzipped, now 5,302 and 1,922, with a script of 3,614 B and 1,643 B gzipped in place of the page script (so its first load is 7.9 KB gzipped, where it was 15.9 KB); the first load of the home page 16.4 KB gzipped, now 18.2 of its 60. The home page of all 560 trios, rendered from the template with made-up entries, is 64.7 KB raw and 8.2 KB gzipped, its first load about 24 KB; the 404 page of all of them is 11.4 KB raw and 3.2 KB gzipped, its first load 9.3 KB.
- 2026-10-19 Wiki is the one evidence level. The user, verbatim: "i think the seen in game vs wiki labels are kinda pointless and likely confusing, lets just trust the wiki and not show a label". Every item that the private seen flags confirm was already at the catalog's wiki level, so no kit loses an item. **The release** builds from the public catalog alone: `--seen`, `--wiki` and `--client` and the catalog with the flags merged (`build_seen_catalog`, `check_seen_catalog`, `snapshot_seen`) are gone, `--privacy-key` and the keyed scan stay, and `check_no_strict_item` refuses a catalog that has any strict item before the first hour is spent, with a message that names `rules/community-seen.json` and no item, so that wiki stays the only level even if a community report is added later (a report in force makes its item strict). With no strict item the strict pool is empty and the precompute, which is not touched, makes the wiki view the default one with its runner-ups and robust picks; the strict shards hold the naked-plus-buffs kits (a trio has 30 chunks in the test world where it had 36, the strict level having no shield setup). **The pages** have no evidence picker, no Seal column in Wear this, no note about strict being empty and no "(none yet)", and say nothing of an evidence level in the headline ("judged in the Balanced view at +4 with your own buffs"), the line of the switches, the notes under the runner-ups or the "not computed" note of the robust core; the item page has no level line (its notes about the item stay). The page code reads the one level it is made from (`data-ev` of the main element, the level of `defaults.json`: wiki in a release) and never writes an `ev`; an `ev` in a link is taken and ignored, and is not malformed, so older links keep working. The browser modes (the best kit, equip now, the farm path, the best sets and the shared kit's value) ask at that same level, so none of them offers a choice of it. The sources and about pages carried no "seen in game" text; the CC BY-SA attribution is as it was. **Left in place, now unused by a release:** the strict machinery of the precompute (the strict pool, its shards, the `--seen` option of `trioforge.precompute`), the community module and its list, and the private seen-flag extractor.
- 2026-10-19 Buffs of equal value are a tie in the shard parity. `tests-js/shard-parity.test.mjs` refused 5 or more views of a real run (for example dru-mnk-wiz wiki/group TRL melee 2h +0) where the engine named another optional buff set than the shard with an equal F, an equal kit and an equal assumed haste: two buffs of equal value. A view whose F, kit and haste are equal and whose buff sets differ now passes (`isBuffTie` of `tests-js/shard-results.mjs`) and is counted and printed as a tie; any difference in F, the kit or the haste still fails, with a difference in the buffs or without it. The buffs line comes last in `compareRow`, so the first difference of a failure is one that matters.
- 2026-10-19 The look of the site is a late-90s EverQuest fan site (the user: "think Allakhazam plus some geocities era images / gifs", "lets really lean on vintage 90's pc asthetic", and not for 800x600 but for "modern 4k/2k/1080p resolutions"). **It replaces the earlier look; there is no switch.** A dark frame with the stone tile; on a window of 56 rem or more a left column with a maroon Navigation bar and the menu as dark plates; maroon bars for the h2; a gold serif h1 in the dark theme and a maroon one in the light (gold on parchment does not hold 4.5:1); the content a column of parchment (stone in the dark theme) under a veil; the gear tables an item window (dark slate, light text, gold heads); square bevelled controls; footer badges. Light and dark both stay, with the same toggle and tokens. The type is `clamp(16px, 10px + .35vw, 21px)`, the frame at most 112 rem and centred, the menu column `clamp(14rem, 14vw, 18rem)`; a phone is one column and keeps the stacked cards. System fonts only (Verdana, and Book Antiqua / Palatino / Times New Roman for the headings). **The art** is pixel art from Retro Diffusion, made into small PNGs by `scripts/art_process.py` (a maintainer's tool: it needs Pillow, which the project does not require, and takes the artist's folder as an argument) from the committed `static/art/manifest.json`; it also writes `static/art/art.css`, which `site.css` imports. esbuild copies each PNG beside the stylesheet under a hash of its content (`--loader:.png=file`), so `img-src 'self' data:` and `style-src 'self'` are enough, and the favicon is the 32 pixel emblem as a `data:` address. Every picture is a CSS background or an element that is `aria-hidden` with no text and no style attribute, shown at a whole multiple of its size (`--art-m` is 2 from 150 rem, a 4K window); every use of a picture has a `var()` fallback, so an asset that is not in the manifest leaves a plainer page. The art of the theme is held to 120 KB by `LIMITS["art"]`. **Motion** is a sprite strip with `steps()`, stopped by the existing reduced-motion rule (it shows frame 1); at most three things move on a page (two torches and the coin on a trio page, the torches and the dwarf on the home page while other trios are not ready), and the mail beside the report link moves only while the link is hovered or has the focus. The torches are not shown on a phone. The source files were not enlarged copies (their scale is 1), so the tool finds a scale of 1 and keeps the size the artist made; sprites are cropped to the box that holds every frame.
- 2026-10-20 The set pieces: scenes and characters on the pages (Phase 6). **What and where.** Scenes are opaque pictures in a bevelled grey "screenshot" frame (`figure.shot`, a raised window around a sunken picture with a status bar for the caption, which is real text; the picture is the `span.art` of the existing mechanism: a CSS background class of `art.css`, `aria-hidden="true"`, no inline style, the policy unchanged), made by the `scene` macro of `base.html.j2`, which draws nothing, caption included, when the asset is not part of the site: the home page has the banner `hero-trio` (192x128) after its first paragraph and the sleeping `dragon` (256x128) after the list of trios; About has `city-gate` (256x96) under the heading; Changelog `campfire` (128x96); the 404 page `treetop-village` (256x128) with the caption "You fell off the platform." in place of the lost picture (its text and links are as they were; `lost` left the manifest). Characters float at the right of their words at 2x with no frame: `scholar` (128x128 cropped to what is drawn) in the first paragraph of Sources, and the race picture inside the line "Best race for this trio". **One table says which race gets which picture:** `art.RACE_PICTURES` (Dwarf, Ogre, Froglok, Iksar, Gnome, Wood Elf; every other race none), read through `art.race_picture(code)`, which is None for a race without a picture or an asset that is not part of the site. The trio page gets `TrioPage.race_picture` from the static best race of the page, never from the race a visitor picks, so the picture does not change when the switch does. **Kerran has no picture.** The only picture of one (`catman.png`) has a small stray animal at its lower left; the two are separate shapes (the figure's box is x 36-92, y 31-147 of its true 128x160, the animal's x 22-41, y 104-117), and any crop that keeps the whole figure starts at x 36 or less and so keeps the animal's pixels from x 36 to 41; a crop that drops the animal cuts the figure. The race stays out of the table rather than show the animal. **The Iksar** is cut off at the shins by the artist's canvas, so its box is the figure's box and its background is anchored at the bottom edge (`.art-race-iksar`). **Scale.** The classes are sized in whole multiples of the true size: scenes 2x, 3x from a 1920 px window (`min-width: 120rem`) and 4x from a 4K one (150rem, where the stylesheet doubles every scale: `--art-s` goes back to 2 there); characters 2x, 4x on 4K. Every picture has a fixed box in CSS (its size times the scale), so nothing moves when it loads. **A phone** (37.5 rem and under) has the scenes at 1x, and three of them are not shown at all (`display: none` on the span that carries the picture and on its frame, so the picture is never fetched): the banner, the gate and the campfire, which would stand in the first screen at 390x844; the dragon is below it and the 404 page's picture takes the place of the one it had at 1x. The scholar of Sources is 1x on a phone; the race picture of the trio page is not shown (`display: none`, so it is never fetched): at 1x it took the width of the best-race line, the kit's first row ended at 797 px of a 740 px phone window, and the page's own test of the first screen (`trio.spec.mjs`, the answer is on the first screen) failed. **The budgets.** The phone budget stays: what a 390 px window fetches on any one page is under 120 KB (the largest page, a trio page, fetches under 40 KB), and `tests-e2e/art-phone.spec.mjs` measures the PNG bytes on the home, a trio, About, Sources, Changelog and the 404 page in the light and the dark theme, and that the hidden banner is not fetched. **The budget of the whole build rises from 120 KB to 200 KB**, because the twelve pictures (about 111 KB of PNG: the largest are the banner at 20 KB, the treetop village at 17 KB, the gate at 15 KB and the dragon at 15 KB) do not fit with the assets of the theme that stay (about 61 KB; the lost picture's 10 KB leaves): 170 KB in all; a phone fetches only what its page draws, and the pictures of one page are a small part of the total, so the figure that decides what a phone pays is the phone's own, and it did not move. It is one constant, `art.BUDGET_BYTES`, which `LIMITS["art"]` of the build and `scripts/art_process.py` both read (the test for the budget holds that they are equal). **The source folder.** The tool takes a flat folder, and the manifest names files, never paths, so the picks of the artist's `r3` and `r4` folders were copied beside the older files into one scratch folder for the run; the new files are made at their true size (the 8x and 4x downloads shrink to it exactly, which the tool checks); the older assets were made again from their sources and are pixel for pixel what was committed (their PNG bytes were left as committed, since another Pillow compresses a little differently). Not used: `brute-try4`, `reddragon`, `hyenamen`, the r4 `campfire`, the first hero, the r3 `iksar`, `ogre` and `dwarf`, `plains` and the adventuring-party picture. **The contract files** `fixtures/site/contract/home-main.html`, `notfound-main.html` and `trio-main.html` were made again on purpose (the frames and the picture in the page).
- 2026-10-20 More art, a shorter menu and a credit (Phase 6, second round; the user approved all three). **The menu** is Trios, About and Changelog: Rules and Sources are out of the left menu (`build._nav`), and both pages stay, with every link to them: the footer's "Sources and licences" link and its "Data: eqlwiki CC BY-SA" badge (the attribution), the trio pages' links into `/rules/`, the data strip's level link, the sitemap. **More scenes.** The plains (`plains`, 256x96) are a banner on the home page above the list of the trios, and the hyena people (`hyenamen`, 192x128) stand beside the treetop village on the 404 page, in a row of frames that wraps (`.scene-row`); both are scenes of the same frame and captions as the others, and a page whose assets are missing has neither the frame nor the row. **The icon of an item** is drawn beside the name on its page (`art.item_icon(slots)`, a span in the heading, `aria-hidden`, in the slot of the item window, 2x from 56 rem): the icon of its place when every place the item fits has the same one, and none when it fits no place, a place without an icon (Hands, Any slot, Off hand), or places with different icons (a main-hand-or-off-hand weapon is not given the sword). **The footer** has two small characters at the ends of the row of badges, the froglok and the bearded warrior (the assets of the race pictures, at their own size, so no new bytes), hidden on a phone and never fetched there. **Nothing else of the first round was moved:** the dwarf miner stays beside the line that says other trios are coming, which is true while trios are unbuilt; no other round-1 sprite was added, since none would be an honest accent (the coin and the two torches already make three moving things on a trio page, the torches and the dwarf on the home page; the mail moves only while its link is hovered). **On a phone** the plains and the hyena people are not shown (the plains would stand in the first screen of a visitor whose browser does not run the picker, which is hidden until it does), so the phone's art is what it was plus the dragon and the 404 page's village. **Scenes are written without an alpha channel.** The seven scenes are opaque, and the tool takes an `"opaque": true` entry as a promise it checks (a transparent pixel is an error) and writes an RGB PNG, which is a fifth smaller and lets the whole art, 196 KB, stay within the 200 KB of the budget with the two new scenes. **The credit:** one plain line in the licences part of the Sources page, "The pixel art was made for TrioForge with Retro Diffusion.", with no logo and no link; the About page has no credits paragraph and says only that data and credits are on the Sources page.
- 2026-10-22 Five rules now backed by public sources (branch p7rules; `rules/rules.json`, the Python model, the browser engine and the parity fixtures changed together). **Weapon damage** is 10 percent of the base a tier with no minimum step: `dmg + dmg * n * pct // 100` (`tier.weapon_damage_pct` = 10, active, 5 stays its listed alternative and the robustness runs try it, now labelled `Weapon damage +5%`; the rule keeps its in-game test id B5, so the robust variant still names it, but the item pages no longer show it as an open test). Sources: the player-client reads at https://xm2514-svg.github.io/sites/item-upgrades/ (Flaming Fist, base 6, reads 8 at +4 and 9 at +5) and https://eqlwiki.com/index.php/Item_Upgrade_System. Delay, the listed damage bonus and the stat rule (`base + max(n, base*n//10)`) are unchanged. **Weight** falls 9 percent of the base a tier with no minimum step: tenths `w` become `max(floor10, w - w * 9 * n // 100)` (new rule `tier.weight_pct` = 9, active; `tier.weight_floor` stays 0.1; weightless stays weightless, unknown weight stays 1.0 stone). Same two sources (1.9 -> 1.8 at +1, 1.0 -> 0.7 at +4, 0.2 -> 0.2 at +5); the item pages link the new rule beside the floor. **Unbound Alacrity** (`haste.unbound_alacrity` = "adds 10 haste and raises the cap by 10", active; sources: the patch notes of 2026-07-28, "an increase in your current and maximum haste value", and https://eqlwiki.com/Haste_Guide, "grants up to 10% haste, and increases the haste cap by 10% as well"): a trio with a Monk has 10 haste that stacks with the spell haste and the best worn haste, up to `haste.cap_pct_with_monk` (85, which already holds the other 10). It is a new last field of the profile, `haste_bonus` (10 with a Monk, else 0; `Profile.FORMAT` stays 1 because the reader already refuses a profile with a missing key), added to the buff haste in the evaluator (`formulas.evaluate` and `evaluate.js`: the `spell` of the haste total) and, so that the two agree, to every spell haste of the MILP (`_term_haste`: the always-on haste and each choice are shifted by it, which leaves the best-one-counts choice as it was) and to `Profile.spell_haste`, which is what the weapon term assumes (`weapons.haste_assumed`, `session_haste`, `hastePercent`); `make_profile` refuses a rule text it does not implement. **Dual-wield factor** by trio (`weapons.dual_wield_factor` = `{with_monk: 0.83, without_monk: 0.69}`, still open under B5): `min(1, best Dual Wield skill / 400 x 1.32)` with the skill at 252 for a Monk and 210 for the other dual wielders and Ambidexterity as the 1.32; the source (https://eqlwiki.com/Skill_Dual_Wield) calls it a working model. `weapons.dual_wield_factor_for(trio, rules)` picks it and `Profile.dw_factor` carries the trio's own, so the bundles hold it in `profile_common`; `weapon_rules.dual_wield_factor` stays the pair in every bundle (the build checks that all bundles share their item rules); a rule that holds one number still applies to every trio, and `damage_index` called with a pair and no factor is refused. **Two-hander damage bonus** with none listed is `0.67 x min(delay, 50)` (https://eqlwiki.com/Game_Mechanics: the bonus counts the delay up to 50); a one-hander keeps `0.5 x delay` (`weapons.BONUS_TWO_HANDED_DELAY_CAP`, exported as `weapon_index.bonus_two_handed_delay_cap`, `evaluate.js` `fallbackBonus`). **Not changed here:** `tier.negatives_scale`, ATK and regen per tier (they wait for in-game readings), the `haste_assumed` coupling, the legacy objective of `solver/compat.py` (its own frozen tier view), the rules file version, and no solver rerun, release or deploy was made: the published results and the release data were made under the old rules and are stale until a run. **The invented fixtures:** with damage growing from the base the weapon rate no longer flipped a kit between 5 and 10 percent in the site fixture, so one invented weapon of the fixture catalog (`w80988`) became 16 damage at a delay of 24;
