the parity, site and shard fixtures were written again the way the repo does (`fixtures.py --sample`, `evaluate_terms.py`, `engine_fixture.py`, `shard_fixture.py`).
- 2026-10-22 The weapon term is paid the haste the kit counts (branch p7haste; `solver/haste.py`, `model.py`, `solve.js`, `model.js`). **The bug.** The weapon index of a hand is `(damage + bonus) / delay * (1 + haste / 100)`, and the builder took one haste for the whole scenario: the best spell haste on offer plus the best worn haste of the pool, whether or not the kit wore that item or took that buff. A haste belt was rejected because the weapons were paid its haste for free, the shown F was above what the kit is worth (1 to 10 percent in the probe of 2026-10-22), and for a trio with no always-on haste (war-clr-mnk) the whole assumed 45 to 55 was phantom. **The fix, exact.** A kit counts one of a few hastes: an active spell haste (the always-on buffs' or one optional haste buff's, each with the 10 of Unbound Alacrity) plus the best worn piece or none, cut at the cap (`haste_levels`, `hasteLevels`). For each level `H` the program is built with `haste_assumed = H`, `haste_floor = H` (the row `haste.floor`: the kit counts at least `H`) and `haste_exact` (the rows `haste.ceil[<spell>]` and `haste.over[<spell>]`: no worn piece above what the active spell haste leaves of `H`, no buff that alone counts more), so the kit counts exactly `H` and the model's value of every kit in it is the evaluator's at the kit's own haste. The best value over the levels is the optimum, because the best kit counts some level and the program of that level holds it. `solve_scenario` (Python) and `solveScenario` (JS) solve the levels best first, by the value their linear relaxation allows, and leave out a level (and all after it) whose relaxation is below the best value found by more than 1e-6 relative; of equal values the kit whose program assumed the haste it counts goes first, then the lower level, and each level's own answer is the canonical one, so the winner does not depend on the order. The answer says the haste the kit counts (`milp.haste_assumed`, the shard's `haste`, `hasteAssumed`), and `formulas.evaluate_truthful` / `evaluateTruthful` value a kit at its own haste. **Unchanged.** An explicit `haste_assumed` option is still one program at that constant, no levels, no rows; a scenario that scores no weapon (the stick, no damage weight) has one program; the bare builder with none still takes the old constant (`weapons.haste_assumed`, now the ceiling of the levels). The pruning is not changed: the order of the weapons of a hand does not depend on the haste, and HASTE is compared as before. **Everywhere the old constant was used.** The precompute (`grid._solve_one`, the unpruned audit, the empty-kit base), the runner-ups (a swapped kit is valued at the haste it counts, which also keeps the solved kit the best there is), the seals and flip checks, the robustness runs, the release spot check, the Upgrade functions (`upgrade.session_haste` is the options' own haste or None, a step says the haste of its own kit, Every slot values each swap at the swapped kit's haste), the value of a shared kit (`kitvalue.js`), the farm path protocol (a step may have another haste than Equip now; the result says Equip now's). The parity fixtures record, for a solve, its levels and the level that won, the options gain `haste_floor` and `haste_exact`, and every fourth brute-force instance is a truthful one with its own brute force (`brute.truthful_instance`). `rules.json` only gets a note on `haste.unbound_alacrity`. **Speed, and what keeps it exact.** The level programs differ from one another only in the weapon coefficients and in the rows after the worn-haste columns, so they are made from one build (`LevelPrograms`, Python and JS: the arrays are those of `build_milp` for the level, bit for bit, which the tests compare). A level's search is given the best truthful value found so far as HiGHS's `objective_bound` (the negative of what the model must reach, the constant left out): HiGHS ends such a search at once and may report a kit it came across that is worth less than the cutoff, so a kit below the cutoff means only that the level cannot win (it is a `bound`, never a candidate) and the tie stage is not run for it; a kit that reaches the cutoff is the level's best, which a test holds on random instances. Only a proof of infeasibility drops a level in its relaxation; a relaxation that stopped, or went wrong, gives no bound and the level is solved. The browser takes the bounds of the levels from one solver whose objective and level rows are changed from level to level, so that the basis of one level starts the next, and makes a model and a solver only for a level that is solved. `evaluate_truthful` values a kit once (every weapon's index is its value at 0 haste times 1 + haste / 100, so the weapon part is scaled to the haste the kit counts; the tests hold it equal to a second evaluation, to the last bit). If every level is infeasible the answer is infeasible, and a level that stopped on its time is never reported as infeasible. The two haste buffs that exclude each other are enforced by the buff set (a pair of haste buffs that can be active together is refused at build time in both engines). **Cost.** On war-clr-mnk (wiki, own, +4, 150 scenarios, 4 processes) the precompute went from 335.6 s (the fixed constant) to 645.2 s (the first levels version) to 412.9 s (this version, 1.23 times the first; every shard, runner-up, default and robustness file is the same as before the speed work, and so is every chunk apart from fingerprints and node counts); the mean solve per scenario was 1.54, 2.09 and 1.91 s. The browser's best kit (median, ms, before, first levels version, now): dual wield own 355, 529, 421 (plain) and 614, 883, 723 (tie stage); one-hander and shield own 646, 1189, 584 and 2082, 3443, 2518; armor class x2 on the one-hander and shield 529, 1786, 819 and 3736, 11823, 3668, the worst setting now 1.55 times the fixed constant. A farm step of the Upgrade tab in Python (3 steps of the real Paladin session): 3.2 s, 15.6 s, 4.5 s. The options not taken: one program with the weapon coefficient chosen per level, and a cap on the levels.
- 2026-10-22 The kit rows say what their items are and why (p7kit). **What.** Each row of "Wear this" has a details element beside its name (the summary is the button "Stats", with aria-expanded and a name that says whose stats; a button "All stats" in the title bar opens every row, disabled and out of sight until the page code starts, like Change): the item's stats at the tier shown (`view.item_stat_text`: stats, regens, worn haste, resists, a weapon as damage/delay and its listed damage bonus, weight), and a why line for the default view. The button is out of the flow (absolutely placed in the cell of the name), so a row is as tall with it as without and the page does not move when the code starts; the details element is closed without script, so the first row is on the first screen of a phone either way. **Where the numbers come from.** The stats are `at_tier` of the item (the figures of the solver, made from the item table by `view.tier_vecs`, the way the item pages are), never worked out in a template or in the browser: the page has them baked for the default view, and every shard carries them for the views it answers (`stat_tiers` and, as the last entry of each item, a text for each tier of the shard), so the page paints them for any view without a second tier implementation. An item of an optimizer answer that no loaded shard lists says that its stats are on its page. **The why.** `precompute/kitwhy.py` values the default kit with `evaluate_truthful` (the kit at the haste it counts) and again with one stat of one item taken away; the loss is the difference of the two exact values as a float (0 past a cap, below 0 for a negative stat; the weapon is the term DAMAGE; CORRUPTION is never scored), kept as `why.places` of `defaults.json` (optional for the reader, so older results still build; the build refuses a why that is not of the kit of the default answer). A loss of 0 carries a reason where it has one (resist, regen or haste cap, haste covered by another piece, AC past the softcap). The line names the three largest losses, what is worth nothing and why, what costs more than it is worth, and the next best item for the place: the first runner-up that needs no deity. **Units** are the hit-point equivalents of the kit value, whole numbers, as the runner-ups' changes are (not percent). Any other view (race, role, tier, buffs, setup, sliders or an optimizer answer) shows the stats and a note that the why lines are for the default view only; the open rows stay open. **Budgets.** The trio page grows by about 9.5 KB raw (1.5 KB gzipped) and the page code by 0.85 KB gzipped, the stylesheet by 0.2 KB; a shard grows by 9 KB raw for a trio of three tiers (2.3 KB gzipped) and by 30 KB raw (5.6 KB gzipped) for the milestone trio of eleven tiers (193 KB of the 200 KB a shard may be). `LIMITS["trio_html"]` rises from 60 KB to 66 KB (a real page was 51.9 KB and is 61.2 KB), and the guard on the contract copy of the page from 44 KB to 52 KB; the shard limits stay. **Measured with** three real trios (read-only copies of the results of data-2026-10-19, with their engine files made again by the current code, and the why made with the real catalog), not a release.
- 2026-10-24 Effects to exalt: the port and its data (part 1, branch p7fx), the page and the browser's re-match (part 2, branch p7fx2); the spec of 2026-10-22, approved on 2026-10-24. **What.** The private analysis is the package `trioforge.effects`: `spells` (the category of a spell, focus limits, what a focus touches), `donors` (the effects of the wiki-level items of the public catalog, each with its donor items), `layer` (what a class needs and the ranked focus, click, carry, worn and proc lists for a trio and role), `plan` (the matching onto a kit), `files` (the documents, their budgets and the writer the release build calls), `derive` (the derived spell file) and `judgment`. With the three fixes and the dispel change reverted, the port gives the analysis' lists for all 560 trios and every offered role (compared once against the private tool's output, outside the tests); the tests hold the two example trios, with every difference from the analysis explained by a fix. **Sources.** Only the client's spell text and the effects the wiki lists on catalog items; no community list is read. The spell text comes through `derived/client/effect-spells.json` (made by `python -m trioforge.effects.derive`: every spell a class gets up to level 50 and every spell named by an effect of a wiki item, 1,779 spells, 271 KB raw and 37 KB gzipped, listed in the client manifest with the same `spells_us.txt` record, whose date is kept; a row has its name only when an item effect of the wiki calls the spell by it, because the others need none and a spell name is a game word the privacy scan can take for a private name). It is read with the row checks, effect-slot reader and value and duration formulas of `sources/client_spells.py`, which is not changed: it is under the code hash of the chunks, and a change there would solve every trio again. **Judgment.** Every constant is one of 21 rules `effects.*` of `rules/rules.json` with the seal model and a note; `Judgment.from_rules` reads them. The rules file is part of the fingerprint of the chunks, so the next release solves every trio again (it would anyway: p7rules and p7haste changed the model). **The three fixes.** (1) A focus keeps partial credit above its maximum level: `max(0, 1 - percent x (level - maximum))` with the percent of the limit effect (Spell Haste II to level 44 at 5 percent: a level-49 spell keeps 75 percent; a maximum with no percent stays a wall), counted in every coverage; the shared file gives each focus its maximum and percent so the page can say "weaker above 44". It moves Reagent Conservation II over III (the easier source is now within 90 percent) and a few tier-II foci a place down. (2) A self-cast that spends hit points for mana is mana, with the mana as its magnitude, not damage or a damage over time: Cannibalize and Cannibalize II of the Shaman, Dark Pact, Allure of Death, Call of Bones and Lich of the Necromancer (the only six among the spells a class gets). Alone it puts Improved Damage III over Burning Affliction II in the caster role of clr-shd-shm; with the partial credit of (1) the order is the analysis' again. (3) The cooldown, cast time and required level are kept on each donor of an effect and not taken from one copy of the text for the group. **The dispel change.** `effects.dispel_relevance` is (0.6, 0.5, 0.7) for a tank, a melee and a caster role, and a dispel is scored by the buffs it strips (Pillage Enchantment 4, Cancel Magic 1) with no cut for the trio's own dispel spell, as the site's default content, difficulty 4, has buffed mobs; with it Pillage Enchantment is in the top 3 clicks of the balanced role of all 560 trios and of the caster role of all 441 trios that offer it (274 of 274 tank and 504 of 505 melee roles too). It is a constant and not a difficulty switch. **Dismiss and Banish.** A damage effect whose spell works on animals, undead or summoned only (target types 9, 10, 11) counts 35 percent of a plain damage effect of its size (`effects.restricted_targets`); that takes Banish Summoned and Banish Undead off the top of a cleric trio's procs too, as they have the same limit. Ykesha stays a small damage proc, as the spell data says, marked unverified (the players call it a hate proc). **The plan.** A recommended effect is placed on an item of the trio's default kit of the role (the default race at the default tier, the best setup) under the rules of the spec: a like item (it shares a slot with the donor; where an item is worn does not matter, so an item in an Any place hosts like any item of its own slot type), a class of the trio that can use both, the socket's tier (the kit's items are all at the kit's tier; a donor that arrives below it is marked as needing an upgrade), a 2H or primary-only donor needs its host in the primary hand itself (not the secondary hand, not an Any place), one effect of a kind for each host, one focus of each stacking type. Two additions, both stated in the code: the host has to be useful (a focus is worth its score times the share of its gain that the classes able to use both items keep, a proc needs a melee class, a mana click a class that casts: otherwise the reason is `class`), and within a kind the placement is the one of the greatest total score (an exact assignment), a tie going to a host that its own donor is and to one a class that gains most can use. An effect with no host goes to the no-room list with a reason: `tier` (the kit is below the socket tier, whatever else is true), `donor` (no copy of the effect can be worn by a class of the trio), `primary`, `slot`, `class` or `taken`. "Any Slot" clicks are the carry list and are not placed. **Marks.** An effect is unverified for four reasons: several spells have the name (`ambiguous`), the wiki does not say whether a click must be equipped (`equip`), a proc inferred from a weapon with no trigger wording (`inferred`), or players describe it differently from the spell data (`description`, Ykesha). **Files.** The release build writes them after the precompute, from the shipped kits and never from the seen flags: `effects.json` beside `items.json` (id, name, kind, category, magnitude, unit, cast level, rate, marks, donors with their cost, and for a focus its family, tier, stacking type, percent range, maximum level, loss a level, limits in words and the share of each class's late spells it touches; and the cast mass of each class, which the re-match needs) and `exalt.json` in each trio's folder (the offered roles only, item keys only: the five lists, for a focus what each trio class adds to its score, the plan rows and the no-room rows). They are in the allowlist of the release and the stage, checked by the release build (the budget, the effect ids, the donors, the hosts, the data release), and the code that writes them is in the writer hash of the release. A trio file may be 20 KB gzipped and the shared file 200 KB raw and 50 KB gzipped. The rules are listed with the ones the solver never reads in `results_diff.UNREAD`, so a change to one explains no change of a pick. A release made before the layer has neither file and the site build does not read them yet: nothing else changes. **Review fixes (2026-10-25).** The foci hold a donor to the required level as the other kinds do; the click scale is measured against the effects the lists can offer (a copy that can be worn, a required level within the cap, a kind the lists treat as a click or worn effect) and the proc scale against the discounted size a proc is scored at, over the procs the lists can offer (inferred ones too); an instrument focus covers only the spells of its own skill (column 32 of the client file, kept as `sk` in the derived rows); the click mode keys a record and the unclear-wording flag is a mark on the copy (a record is unverified for `equip` when any copy has it); an unlabelled offensive effect is a proc on a weapon copy and a click on any other, as two records, never one mixed record; "Recast Time" is not a cast time; an item with no slot list has no slot; `write_release` removes an `exalt.json` it did not make this time, and a release built without the layer removes the effects files of an earlier run before it stages; `check_release` refuses a file that is JSON but not of the form, by name. In the example trios this moves the carry, worn, click and proc lists listed in the report (no focus list): the two scales, nothing else. The data has no record that the flag or the inference split. **Measured** with three real trios whose default kits the current code solved for three races at +4 (not a release): `exalt.json` of pal-mnk-ber 10.8 KB raw and 1.3 KB gzipped (three roles), clr-shd-shm 15.6 KB and 2.1 KB (four roles), wiz-mag-enc 4.9 KB and 1.3 KB (one role); `effects.json` 69.8 KB and 10.5 KB. With made-up kits of real catalog items for all 560 trios, the largest trio file is 20.6 KB raw and 2.6 KB gzipped (shd-brd-mag), against the 20 KB gzipped budget. **Part 2: the page.** The section is a closed `details` at the end of the trio page, called a planning aid, a shell of about 0.5 KB that is not there when the results have no effects files (a trio without its own file has none either). Its code is one chunk, `ui/exalt.js` with the matcher `ui/exalt-plan.js`, which the build refuses to put in the page script (`check_exalt_chunk`); the page script only binds the summary (aria-expanded), and loads the chunk, which fetches the two files, when the section is opened or its summary comes within 300 px of the window: nothing before, so the first view and the phone budget are what they were (the page script is 0.6 KB gzipped larger, the page 0.2 KB, the stylesheet not at all: the styles of the section are a constructed stylesheet the chunk makes, which the content security policy does not govern). The site reader (`data.load_exalt`) holds the files to the item table, the run, the default answer (the plan is made for its race and tier), the budgets and the vocabulary, and the build publishes `data/effects.json` (the shared file, with for each donor its name, whether it has a page, its slots, classes, two-hand flag and arrival tier, and the late spell level of the rules) and `data/<trio>/exalt.json` (with the names of the items the plans put an effect on, since the page has no keys for the kit it is built with), counted in `site_files`, the file budget of the release check and `check_budgets`. **The re-match.** The plan in the file is shown for the kit it was made for (the default race, tier, buffs, weights and the role's best setup); for any other kit of Wear this (another race, tier, setup or buffs, an answer of the optimizer) or the kit of Equip now in the Upgrade section (a second source, chosen with a pair of radio buttons that is there only when Equip now has a kit), the plan is matched again in the browser by `ui/exalt-plan.js`, a port of `effects/plan.py`: the same rules, the same exact assignment (the Hungarian method on whole numbers, the first column winning among equal totals), the same weights, with Python's `round` (a half goes to the even number) and `sum` (the compensated sum of 3.12). It follows the rules of the review round of part 1: a 2H or primary-only donor needs its host in the PRIMARY place itself (not the off hand, not an Any place), a kit below the tier of a socket gives `tier` to every effect of that kind, an effect no class of the trio can wear a copy of gives `donor`, and the fixtures were made again from the fixed Python. `tests-js/exalt-plan.test.mjs` holds it to `fixtures/effects/plan-parity.json`, made by `python -m trioforge.effects.fixtures` (kits of real catalog items, seeded, at six tiers, for 23 trios and every role they are offered, with the rows and the no-room list Python gives), and `pipeline/tests/test_effects_fixtures.py` holds the file to the code that makes it. A host has a tier of its own in the browser (a copy the visitor owns may be below the kit's), which Python's plan, made for one tier, does not need; with one tier for all it is the same plan. The lists do not depend on the kit and are never made again. The item list the optimizer's worker reads is fetched once, from the path `engine-manifest.json` names, for the hosts of a re-matched kit of Wear this (the donors are in `effects.json`, the kit of Equip now brings its own records); when it cannot be had the page says so and keeps the plan of the file. **Words.** A focus reads "15% faster casts for all spells with a cast time of 2 s or more ... up to spell level 44 (weaker above)" with a note that spells above 44 lose 5% a level, a coverage chip ("touches 83% of what your Paladin casts at 30+"), the socket it opens at, the best source with the others and how hard each is to get (a dot and the words vendor, quest or crafted, drop, raid drop), and a why-not note for the families that lost the type; the other kinds read their meaning from the spell data (damage, healing, mana, haste ...). A row built on an assumption has an `unverified` button that opens its reason. **Sources.** The sources page names `spells_us.txt` as the source of what an effect does and says that the effects items carry are wiki data under the same credit and licence; the attribution text itself is unchanged (it would change every data file and `LICENSE-DATA`). `release.json` does not record the hash of `effect-spells.json`: its fields are fixed and a new one is a change of the release format, and `effects.json` carries the SHA-256 of `spells_us.txt` (`spells_us`) while the code hash of the release covers the effects code and the committed `derived/client/effect-spells.json` is part of the commit the release is made from, so the hash would say nothing the release does not already.
- 2026-10-25 EQ Legends Tools is the source of truth for the items of the zone pages we read from it (branch p7nek; the user's decision of 2026-10-25, first for the 46 items of https://eqlegendstools.com/zones/nektropos-castle/). **What.** `sources/eqlegendstools.py` fetches the zone page and each `/items/<slug>/` page it links (the shared polite client: one request a second, only `/zones/<slug>/` and `/items/<slug>/` addresses, which `allowed_url` and the client's `allow` function hold, no redirect followed; the pages are cached raw under `sources/eqlegendstools/<date>/raw/`, so a rerun of the folder asks for nothing it has; `eqlwiki fetch --eqlegendstools-out DIR` runs it after the wiki's fetch), and reads the tooltip with the statsblock parser the wiki's items go through. **In the catalog** (`python -m trioforge.data.catalog --eqlegendstools DIR`, `data/eqlt_merge.py`) the tooltip overrides the wiki's copy of the same item (slots, classes, stats, resists, flags, effects, weapon fields, level fields, weight, size and drops; found by the name compared as the evidence ladder compares titles, apostrophes ignored; the key, the wiki page, quests, vendors, recipes, categories, era tags and the wiki page's banners stay), an item the wiki lacks is added with the key `rlt-<slug>`, no wiki page and `origin` `{source, slug, overrides_wiki}`, and a field the page does not show stays unknown (the wiki's value for a copy, null for a new item; nothing is made up). Each such item has the note `source-eql-tools` (seal player) among its evidence reasons, the summary lists every field where the two disagree, and the sources manifest and the provenance name the site. **The zone.** The client does not flag Nektropos Castle (zone 28), so `rules/hand-lists.json` (`exists_unflagged_zones`, seal player, the zone page as the source) and `rules/zone-aliases.json` now say that it exists in EQL; without that every item here would be hidden for `zones-not-in-eql`. **On the site** an item page shows a Source credit with the link to its page there, and the sources page names the site, links it and lists the items; the attribution text of the wiki (`build.ATTRIBUTION`, the footer, LICENSE-DATA) is as it was, and the sources page says that the site's data is not covered by the wiki's CC BY-SA licence. The weekly `scripts/sources_check.py` never asks this site (`test_sources_check.py` holds that script to the wiki and EQL Tools). **Natural Durability** (part 2 of the same branch, approved 2026-10-25): rule `hp.natural_durability_pct` (ranks 2, 5, 10 and 12 percent; sources: the client's `dbstr_us.txt`, AA 107 type 4, and the eqlwiki AA data of revision 151303 as eqlbuilds.com extracted it; seal player), rank 4 assumed for every class as Combat Stability 3 is; its percent goes on the `fac` of every HP pool in `make_profile` (so the evaluator, the builder and the browser, which all read the pools, agree by construction), `Profile.nd_pct` and `aa.natural_durability` record it, and the game window (`stat_window`, `nd_pct`) floors the STA part and then the scaled part (245 becomes 274). The version label of `rules/rules.json` stays 2026-10-13, as in every earlier rule change; the results are tied to the rules by the hash of the file, so every shard must be solved again. **Fix round p7nekfix (2026-10-27, after review of the merge).** (1) `release-build.py` refuses, before any solving, a catalog whose provenance does not name a zone page of this site that `rules/hand-lists.json` (`exists_unflagged_zones`) takes its items from (`release.check_eqlegendstools_source`): `--eqlegendstools` is optional in the catalog builder, and a catalog built without it still has the zone, with the wiki's numbers. (2) The evidence reason of a live source in an exists-unflagged zone carries that entry's own seal and, when it is not the developers', its source address (the Nektropos entry is sealed player; the entries of the developers read as before). (3) A tooltip page with no flags line keeps the wiki's flags and lists `flags` as unknown (Lore is never dropped for want of a line). (4) A new item whose tooltip states no Class or no Slot line is left out of the catalog and listed under `skipped` in the summary, with a build warning (the parser's default of all classes would be made up). (5) The weapon is merged field by field like the scalars; a field the tooltip omits keeps the wiki's value and is listed as `weapon.<field>` among the unknown. (6) A name on two wiki pages is a build error naming the item, no longer an added `rlt-` copy beside both. (7) A `Proc Rate: -75%` line of the site goes into its effect line as the wiki writes it (`Effect: X (Combat, Rate -75%) at Level 45`), and `donors.parse_raw` reads the sign of a rate (before, only `Rate +N%` filled `rate_pct`, so the wiki's `Rate -75%` filled nothing; no other item of the catalog has a negative rate). The site's `at Level 45` is read as a cast level, as on every other tooltip; the wiki copy of that item had written `Req Level 45`, so its effect record differs (cast level 45, no required level) and is the site's. (8) The hash of the fetched item pages is made from the text with the line ends normalised, as it is read back, so a page sent with CRLF cannot fail the check on reading. **Latent findings p7latent (2026-10-28).** (3) now also holds for a flags line the parser cannot read (it keeps the wiki's flags and lists `flags` as unknown unless a flag was read or the line says none); (5) a tooltip that shows no weapon field but states a type that is no weapon (`Type: Shield`) drops the wiki's weapon; (7) a `Proc Rate` line after an effect with no bracket gets its own `(Rate N%)`; the release check compares the zone page address in one form on both sides (scheme, host case, `www.`, trailing slash); skipped pages keep their parse warnings in the report; the `mana.wis_bands` flip phrase is back.
- 2026-10-27 The mana curve of WIS and INT is one slope from 200 to 510 (branch p7mana; `rules/rules.json`, the Python model, the browser engine, the goldens and the parity fixtures changed together). **The reading.** A maintainer's in-game stat window reading, with no buffs, of a level 50 character whose two counted mana pools are both WIS pools: 10 more WIS above 255 (WIS 270 -> 280) added 118 max mana. The old rule `mana.wis_bands` (breaks 100/200/255/510, slopes 1/2.5/1.25/0.625/0, EQEmu's code) predicted 56 and its alternative (0.654 above 200, from https://eqltools.com/attributes) about 59; a continuous 1.25 from 200 to 510 predicts exactly 118 through the window's floors (for pools of factor 4.5): converted 437.5 -> 450, `floor(437.5) = 437`, `floor(4.5 x 437) = 1966` against `4.5 x 450 = 2025`, 59 a pool, 118 for two. **The rule.** `mana.wis_bands` is breaks [100, 200, 510], slopes [1, 2.5, 1.25, 0]; seal `measured`, status `active`, date 2026-10-27, sources: EQEmu (the first two bands) and the fixed private label (the schema allows nothing else for a private reading, and the notes keep digits out), so the reading itself (the trio, the totals) is not in the repo. The test id B4 and the alternative are gone (B4 is done, and the 0.654 reading is ruled out), the way `haste.unbound_alacrity` lost B8 when it was settled, so the rules page lists B4 no more, and the robustness runs lose the variant `WIS band 0.654` (14 variants for a trio with everything; `robust.py` still makes `WIS band <slope>` from an alternative that a rules file lists, and `PHRASES` lost its mana entry). **The code.** `formulas.MANA_BANDS` and the engine's `CONSTANTS.manaBands` (`evaluate.js`) are the new curve; `export/browser.py` writes `mana_bands` from `MANA_BANDS`, so the bundles follow. The 255 knee stays the stat knee (STA for HP, DEX past a tenth of a point); it is no break of the mana curve. **The tests.** The measurement is `test_ten_wis_above_255_adds_the_mana_of_the_continuous_curve_in_the_stat_window` (Cleric/Shaman/Warrior, WIS 270 -> 280 = exactly 118); the JS parity case is the new term `mana-wis-curve` of `fixtures/evaluate-terms.json` (two WIS pools at WIS 200, 255, 270, 280 and 510, held to Python exactly, and 12.5 converted points x 2 pools x 4.5 = 112.5 exact between 270 and 280, the window's floors giving the 118). The five goldens of the ERU CLR/WIZ/ENC trio above 255 (`formulas-pools.json`, `formulas-stat-window.json`) were worked again by hand from the new curve, not from the model. The 0.654 curve stays in the tests only as an invented custom curve for `bands=`. **Not changed here:** the rules file version label (`2026-10-13`: no rule change has moved it since it was made; the rule's own date says what changed), the `brute` generator's random profile bands (an invented curve for variety, not a rule), no solver rerun, release or deploy: the published results were made under the old curve and are stale until a run.
- 2026-11-08 Racial abilities are shown on the trio page and never scored (branch race-explain; the user's request to explain what each close race gets you). The data is `derived/racials.json`, made from the race pages of eqlwiki by `trioforge.sources.racials` and read by the build (`--racials`); the model scores the Iksar's natural AC and no other racial, and the page says so beside every racial ("not scored", and "scored" with the rule's number for the Iksar's AC). A race snapshot is filed in `sources/eqlwiki/races/<date>`, never beside the pulls of items. Slam and every other racial come from the wiki's own page of the race and from nothing else. The chip of worn haste in a row says the figure the Stats panel says; the note "Only the highest worn haste counts" is the model's rule for worn haste.
- 2026-11-09 The weight rule is now backed by in-game readings, and the site says that stats change between tiers (branch weight-open; `rules/rules.json`, the rules page, the item pages and the trio page; no formula, model, engine or results change, and no rerun). **The rule.** `tier.weight_pct` keeps its value (9 percent of the base weight a tier), its formula (tenths of a stone, `max(floor, w - w*9*n//100)`, no minimum step) and its status; its seal is now `measured` and its one source the fixed private label, the way `mana.wis_bands` is, so its notes keep digits out and say the formula in words, name the items that were read, say that progress toward the next tier lowers the weight further in proportion, and leave one point open: the rounding of the last tenth of a stone is not settled. The readings themselves (the figures) are not in the repo. **Why the sources changed.** The two public pages the rule cited (the player-client page of item upgrades and the wiki's upgrade page) did not support the formula, and neither states the weight rule: one of them lists a weight of 1.0 as 0.8 at +2, which the formula does not give (0.9), and which progress toward the next tier would explain, or nothing does. The wiki page stays the source of `tier.weight_floor` and both pages stay the sources of `tier.weapon_damage_pct`, which this line does not touch. **What the readings say.** The weights read in game on several items fit the formula at their tier, and they show one thing more: an item's stats and weight change gradually with its progress toward the next tier, not only at whole tiers. The model and the site keep to whole tiers: every figure on every page is for an item exactly at a tier, and the rules page, the item pages and the trio page now say so in one sentence ("An item's stats and weight change gradually as it progresses toward its next tier. Each item is shown exactly at its tier."). Modelling progress between tiers is not done here, and the rounding of the last tenth of a stone, which the readings between tiers leave open, stays open. **Not changed:** the rules file version label (`2026-10-13`, as on 2026-10-27: the results were made under it and carry that version and a fingerprint of the catalog and its evidence levels, and nothing of a rule's seal, sources, date or notes, so they stay valid without a rerun; the rule's own date says what changed), the results, the release pointer and the deploy.
- 2026-11-10 Spend your motes: the order in which to spend void-touched motes on a kit (branch mote-order; `trioforge/motes.py`, `static/engine/motes.js`, `static/ui/motes.js`, `static/engine/view.js`, the rule `tier.void_mote`, `fixtures/site/mote-orders.json`; no formula of the model, no solver, no result and no rerun changes). **The rule.** A void-touched mote raises any one item by one whole tier, at any tier, up to +10, with no limit on the motes one item takes (read in game by the maintainer on 2026-11-10). `tier.void_mote` is in the tier group, value one whole tier a mote, sealed `measured` with the one fixed private label, dated 2026-11-10, its notes without a digit (the form `tier.weight_pct` has); the rules file's version label stays `2026-10-13` (the results carry that version and the fingerprint of the catalog, not a rule's seal, date or notes, so they stay valid: the real results build the site as before and no release check refuses them), and `reports/results_diff.py` lists the rule with the ones the solver never reads (the site reads it). **The definition (Python is the truth; JS is a port held to it by a recorded fixture, as `engine/path.js` is held to `upgrade.py`).** `mote_order(profile, kit, records, rules, setup, buffset, buffs, motes)`: the kit is fixed (the same items in the same places, each at its own tier; only tiers change; two worn copies of one item are two items). A step is a RUN (the fix round of 2026-11-10, after a review found that a gain can sit two or more motes away on one item: a weapon whose damage rises only every second tier, a haste that counts only once it passes the best worn one, so that the one-tier rule ranked such an item at nothing and could end "no-gain" while something gains). For each worn item below +10 its run is the smallest k from 1 such that raising it k tiers (the rest of the kit as it stands) changes the kit value by more than nothing, and its rate is that gain divided by k, exactly; an item that gains at no k up to +10 has no run; only an item whose one-tier gain is not above nothing is looked at past k = 1, so the common case costs what it did. The kit is valued under the value the Upgrade tab uses everywhere (`evaluate_truthful`: the weapons are paid the haste the kit counts; the view's role, race, weights and weapon setup; the optional buffs the kit was made with held fixed, as Every slot holds them). A step takes the run with the highest rate (equal rates: the shorter run, then the place earlier in the kit's order) and applies the whole run: ONE line of the list, k motes spent (the lines are numbered by the list; the motes spent so far are said on the lines once they differ from the line's number). With a limit a run longer than the motes left is not eligible, so a number of motes is an order of its own and not a cut of the whole one. It ends when every item is at +10 (`top`, and an empty kit), when the motes asked for are spent or no eligible run fits in what is left (`motes`, with `next_run` the length of the run that did not fit, or none when the limit was reached), or when no item has a run (`no-gain`, which is now true: nothing that is left gains, at any length; it says how many motes could still be spent). In this model a one-tier raise never lowers the value (every stat of an item only rises with its tier and its weight only falls, and the value falls with neither a stat nor a lighter kit; a property test over 2,430 raises in random instances of the solver's own tests finds none), so every gain is at least nothing; the definition says "more than nothing" all the same and a made-up evaluator with a loss on the way is tested. It is greedy: the best single next step, not a proven best plan for N motes, and the page says so in plain words. Each step carries the place, the item, the tiers, the motes it takes and spent so far, the exact gain of the run, the exact kit value after it and the stats that moved (the kit totals the evaluator counts, from AC and the pools to the five resists and the regens, in a fixed order, each by its exact change, and `DMG`, the damage of the item itself when it is a weapon). The module is outside `model/` and `solver/` (the code hash of a release hashes those), so the release check sees no change. The recorded cases (20 kits of the synthetic site fixture: Equip now's kits with a limit, the small kits in full, one whose last motes gain nothing, kits under drawn weights, one with every item at one tier, the kit of the baked page, the kit Equip now finds for the browser test's gear, one a tier from the top and one at the top, and five that are made of three invented items the shards do not show and whose records are in the fixture file: the damage plateau, the haste overtake in full, with eight motes, with three (the run of six does not fit) and a head piece taking the three instead) are made by `pipeline/tests/engine_fixture.py` and held to the page's code exactly (`tests-js/motes.test.mjs`); the page's own exact search for the best optional buffs of a kit it shows (`bestBuffsOfKit`) finds the buffs Python's solver chose for each of them. **Where it shows (both).** One panel, `#upgrade-motes`, after the answer and under one check box, `#upgrade-motes-open` (the one new element of the template: 143 bytes of HTML in the largest trio page, which keeps 400 bytes of its 66 KB in the build with an owner that was measured, 543 before; the box is one of the controls that wake the section's code, and the box's own state is the panel's, so a click before the code is there is not lost). The panel is for the gear (the kit Equip now wears, at the owned tiers, under the request it was worked out for, its weights and its buffs: `upgrade-answer.js` hands it over with `onGear`, takes it back the moment the gear, the view or the tier mode changes, and hands it over again when a change is undone) or for the best kit of the page (the published kit Wear this shows, every item at the tier of the view, read from the rows of Wear this, since the page script knows no kit while the baked answer is shown and must not grow; offered only for the own and group buffs with the role's own weights, where Wear this shows a published kit and not the optimizer's answer; its optional buffs are the best choice for it, which the page does not have). An optional whole number of motes limits the list (empty: all; any whole number from 1, whatever its length, is a number, and more than there are to spend means the whole order; each number is worked out once and kept for the kit on the page). The order is worked out with the buffs this kit has now, and the panel says so in one sentence in plain words; for the page's kit the buffs are the best choice the exact search finds, and when that search ran out of calls the panel says that they may not be the best ones and still gives the order (it does not withhold the published source). A line reads "Chest: <item> +7 to +8, +142" (a run: "Dagger: <item> +0 to +2, 2 motes, +1"), then the stats that moved and the kit value after the step (and the motes spent so far, once a run has made them differ from the number of the line); the fixed sentence "A mote gives one whole tier at any tier, so it saves the most work on the highest tiers." is in the panel, and the sentence about stats between tiers is not said again. **Where it runs.** On the main thread in slices of 12 ms, by the evaluator's script (`engine/kitvalue.js`, which a shared kit loads: by the path, size and SHA-256 of the manifest, then imported; it needs no solver, worker, download of the optimizer or question), with a quiet line of progress, ended by any change of the kit, the view or the source (a run has a number, and an older one is never drawn: the list goes at once and nothing of it comes back). Measured on the real results (22 items from +0, the worst case: 210 steps of 22 evaluations at most, more for an item that has no run, which is looked at to the top): 0.11 to 0.15 s in Node for the walk (0.13 before the look-ahead), 0.27 to 0.30 s in Chromium from the click to the end of the list (the script, the bundle and the exact buff search included), and 3.55 s under the CPU throttle of DevTools at rate 6 (the phone gate's proxy, harsher than a phone 6 times slower than the desktop: the longest task is 0.32 s, once, for the script, the bundle and the buff search; the slices are 12 ms). Nothing is stored, sent or tracked. **Budgets** (real results, as built with an owner for release): first-load script unchanged (`app.js` 45,403 bytes raw before and after, byte for byte the same size; the stylesheet and the first load of a trio page unchanged); the Upgrade chunk 74,625 to 87,985 bytes raw and 27,829 to 32,462 gzip (limits 100 KB and 40 KB); all the code loaded on first use 136.9 to 149.9 KB (limit 250 KB); the evaluator of a shared kit 65,431 to 77,394 bytes raw and 22,492 to 26,115 gzip (limits 80 KB and 28 KB: it now has 4.4 KB raw and 2.5 KB gzip to spare, since it carries the profile of a view with its weights and the order of motes with its runs); the optimizer's worker +4 bytes (the profile of a view moved to `engine/view.js`, which `bestkit.js` re-exports; `tests-js/engine-weighted-modes.test.mjs` follows it there). Edits to existing tests, on purpose: the expected rule ids and sources of `test_rules.py`, the list of the rules the solver never reads in `test_results_diff.py`, the files `test_site_fixture_items.py` scans for real item names, and the place `engine-weighted-modes.test.mjs` looks for `viewProfile`; the contract copies of both trio pages are made again (the box, one line), and `mountAnswer` of the answer's tests takes an optional `onGear`.
- 2026-11-11 Item icons: a picture that fits the item, not only the place (branch item-icons; `site/itemicon.py`, `site/art.py`, `scripts/art_process.py`, `static/art/`, `site/data.py`, `site/view.py`, `site/build.py`, `shard.js`, `trio.js`, `ui/weights.js`; the reviewer's fix round of 2026-11-12 and the second round that replaced the rule of the place's picture are in this entry). **Which picture.** One function, `itemicon.icon_name(item)`, says it, from what the catalog says of the item and nothing else; a rule written twice would be two rules, so the pages, the data files and the browser all take its answer. Its guide is that a wrong picture is worse than a general one, and a general one worse than none where even that would be a guess. **The name says what an item is by its head word**: the last word of a part of the name before an `of`, a `with`, a comma or a parenthesis, and a part after a comma is read as well (`Crookstinger, Axe of Pain` is an axe); a word is a run of letters of any alphabet, never a piece of a longer one (the `axe` of Saxen, the `staff` of Falstaff, the `axe` of Éaxe are not words). A head word names **an object the art has a picture of** (sword, axe, hammer, mace, dagger, staff, wand, spear, bow, fist, book, orb, instrument: `OBJECT_WORDS`), **an object it has none of** (halberd, scythe, naginata, voulge, glaive, whip, claw, pick, rod, stick, bo, shovel, censer ...: `DENIED_WORDS`), or nothing the lists know (a name of its own: Windstriker). **A weapon** (its skill is one of `WEAPON_SKILLS`; the catalog wrote `1H Slash` and `1H Slashing /` badly and they give none) gets the picture of the object its name says, **whatever its skill** (an Orb of Tishan is an orb though its skill is one-handed blunt; the Warstaff of Hope's Demise is a staff though its skill is piercing; a sword, an axe and a hammer are the one-handed or two-handed picture as the skill's hands say); a name that says an object the art has no picture of gives **none** (not the skill's: a scythe is not a two-handed sword), and so do two different objects; a name that says nothing known gets the skill's picture (1H slashing a sword, 2H slashing a two-handed sword, 1H blunt a mace, 2H blunt a hammer, piercing a dagger, 2H piercing a spear, hand to hand a fist, a thrown weapon the thrown picture, archery a bow). **Something held that is no weapon** (it fits a hand or the range place) is a wand, a book, an orb or an instrument by its head word, an instrument by the catalog's instrument modifiers, and a shield by the catalog's `item_type` (never by name: the catalog vetoes some; the catalog's word holds for a weapon too); two of these say none, and the words of weapons say nothing of a thing that is no weapon. The head word, not any word of the name, because any word made a Flute Mouthpiece and an Unfinished Lute Neck instruments. **Armor** that fits exactly one of head, chest, arms, legs, feet and hands gets that part in the material of the catalog's `Fashion: Plate`, `Leather`, `Chain` or `Cloth` category, **but the category is an appearance, not a material**, so it is used only where the name does not contradict it (a name that says a material, chain, mail, ringmail, plate, platemail, leather, hide, silk, cloth, robe or scale, must say the category's alone) and the head word is a shape the picture of that part in that material shows (`ARMOR_SHAPES`: the cloth head is a hood, so no crown, circlet, tiara, headband, helm, helmet, coif, mask or flower of the cloth category gets it; the cloth chest is a robe or a tunic, so no breastplate); the other fashion categories (an era, a class) are not read; of the 1,417 armor pieces the category alone would have pictured, 235 are sent to the place's picture by the name (199 by the shape, 36 by another material in the name). **The picture of a row of the kit and of the page of an item** (`art.row_icon`, `art.item_icon`; the maintainer's rule of the second round, which replaced a rule that showed the place's picture only where every place the item fits had it, and left the Wrist row of Back Straps of Mastery, which fits the back and the wrist, with none): **a worn place** (every armor and jewelry place: head, face, ear, neck, shoulders, arms, back, wrist, hands, finger, chest, legs, feet, waist) shows the item's own picture, else the place's, always, whatever other places the item fits; **a held place** (the main hand, the off hand, the range and the any slots: `art.HELD_PLACES`) shows the item's own picture and nothing else, so that no sword or bow stands in for an item that is neither (the Soldier's Brooch of the Virtuous, in the range, was shown a bow); the sword and the bow are the own pictures of swords and of archery weapons, and the held places are not in `art.SLOT_ICONS`; **an own picture of armor stays in the place of its own part** (a chest picture is never on the legs row: another worn place has its own place's picture instead; the rule already gives armor a picture only to an item that fits no other place, and this holds the row to it whatever the data says); **the page of an item** shows its own picture, else the place's picture when every place it fits is a worn place and they all have the same picture, else none (a thing for two worn places of two pictures, or for a held place, has a heading without one). The hands have a picture (`icon-hands-leather`, which stays the picture of the Hands row for a hands item that has no material picture: decided). So a row needs only the item's own picture and its place, and the shards and the list carry no third thing. The cell of a place with no picture keeps the room of one (a clear box, `site.css`, `:has()`; a browser without it moves the rows below by the difference). The generic sword cell (`icon-sword`, once the main hand's picture) was drawn by nothing and is no longer in the sheet; the bow cell stays as the own picture of the bow. Of the 6,820 items of the release table, 2,806 have an own picture; of the other 4,014, 3,541 fit a worn place (their rows there have the place's picture) and 473 fit held places only (none); the page heading has the own picture for 2,806, the place's for 3,510 and none for 504. The pouch of the new set is not used: no rule gives an item a pouch, and a picture nothing draws is bytes for nothing. The Upgrade section draws no picture today and none was added. **The catalog gate.** The item table of the results has no categories and no instrument modifiers, so the build reads `derived/catalog.json` for them (`--catalog`; `build.load_icon_extras`) and **stops with an error** when the SHA-256 of that file is not the one the item table's `inputs` fingerprint is made of (the release's pointer, `releases/<release>.json`, records the catalog's hash, and a test holds the file to it): a build that went on without the catalog would drop every armor and instrument picture with only a warning. The one way to build without it is to say so, `--no-item-catalog` (it prints a warning and cannot be given with `--catalog`); the synthetic fixture was not made from the real catalog, so the previews and the tests use it, and **a release does not**. A build made from the catalog also stops when the table has pieces of armor and none of them got a material picture. Every icon name the build writes is checked against the browser's pattern (`data.ICON_NAME`) and a data file may name 62 at most, each a `BuildError`. **Where the data is.** A shard carries three fields, `icons` (the names it uses), `ic` (one character for each item: its own picture, the name's place in `icons` or `-`) and `pl` (one for each of the 22 places, `-` for a held one): 0.4 KB more in the largest shard (191.3 to 191.7 KB of 200 KB). The rows an answer of the optimizer draws have items from the whole item table, not from the shard, so the site publishes `data/item-icons.json` (`ic` for each item of the table in the table's order, the SHA-256 of the table's file, the same `icons` and `pl`: 7.9 KB raw and 3.1 KB gzipped, fetched with the table and only then; its own budget, 16 KB and 6 KB gzipped, `LIMITS["icons"]`); the chunk of the weights reads it through the page script's `readIcons` (a chunk may not import what the page script does), and a list that is for another table or release, that is not there or that is not what the generator writes costs the items' own pictures and nothing else: an item of an answer is then known by none of its own, so a worn place still shows its own picture (the page never takes one from a worn place) and a held place none. No JavaScript port of the rule of which picture an item has; the browser keeps only the rule of the row (`rowIcon`: own, else the worn place's, a held place's own only, an armor picture in its own part's place), which `art.row_icon` says too and the tests hold to each other, and reads characters the generator wrote and refuses what it did not (`parseIcons`: names are `icon-` and letters, digits and hyphens, so no markup can reach a class), so there is nothing to hold to the Python but the data, which `pipeline/tests/test_site_icons.py`, `tests-js/item-icons.test.mjs` and `tests-e2e/item-icons.spec.mjs` do. **The art.** The new set (41 pictures used: 17 held, 24 of armor, the originals and the artist's second tries of the one-handed axe and of the staff) and the old icons that are still drawn are one sheet, `icons.png` (`kind: sheet` in the manifest: 55 cells of 32x32 in rows of 8, a clear gap of 2 pixels after every cell so that no neighbour is drawn into a cell at a fractional pixel ratio, a `use` line for each cell), written by `art_process.py` with a palette of 255 colours cut with a tolerance of 12 of 255 in any channel (the real worst is 10, the mean colour distance 1.4: the tool refuses a cut over the tolerance, and a side-by-side of the sheet and its sources shows no difference). `art.css` has one rule for all the cells, `.art-icon-helm,.art-icon-face,{...}`, and for each cell only `{--art-cx:3;--art-cy:1}`; the markup of a row is what it was (`<span class="art art-icon-chest-plate" aria-hidden="true">`), so the page grows only by the longer names (the largest trio page 66,828 to 67,034 bytes of the 67,584 that 66 KB is: the limit is unchanged). No test holds the bytes of a picture (another Pillow writes other bytes for the same pixels), only its pixels. The froglok, the iksar and the hyena people are the new drawings under the same asset names; the first two are written with a palette too (86 and 74 colours: the same pixels in 5,143 and 3,868 bytes, where RGBA was 14,538 and 10,192), and the hyena people stay opaque RGB, which the scenes are. **The art budget rises from 200 KB to 210 KB** (`art.BUDGET_BYTES`, which `LIMITS["art"]` and `art_process.py` both read): the art was 200,906 bytes and is 211,427 (the sheet is 17,483 bytes where the fifteen single icons were 8,098, and the three drawings are 33,510 bytes where the old ones were 32,374), and 210 KB leaves 3.6 KB. What a phone fetches on one page stays under 120 KB (`art-phone.spec.mjs` holds it); the other budgets hold (first-load JS 15.4 to 15.9 KB gzipped of 30, the first load of a trio page 37.5 to 38.2 KB of 60). Edits to existing tests, on purpose: the art budget (`test_art_process.py`, `test_site_build.py`), the manifest tests that read a `source` of every asset (a sheet has the sources of its cells), `png_pixels` (it reads a PNG with a palette too), the places that had no icon (`NO_ICON`, the Hands row), the rows and the item pages that now have the item's picture, the three file counts of `test_site_files.py` (one file more), the tests of the places' pictures in `test_site_art.py` (the main hand and the range have none now), the shard and data-file tests and the three command-line builds of the fixture of `test_site_build.py` (`--no-item-catalog`), the rule of `test_site_theme.py` that every art rule is pixelated (a cell's rule only says where it is, and the shared rule is checked to cover every cell), the floor of the pictures `test_release_checks.py` reads (the floor was 40 files and the art is 28 files now: the fifteen old icons are the cells of one sheet), `tests/conftest.py` (a session fixture builds the fixture results without the item catalog unless a test says otherwise) and `scripts/preview.mjs` (the same flag for the fixture), and the contract fixtures of the trio page and its shards.
- 2026-11-13 Held icons: thirteen pictures for the things held, and the pouch for a row of a held place that has none (branch held-icons; `site/itemicon.py`, `site/art.py`, `site/view.py`, `site/build.py`, `site/data.py`, `static/trio.js`, `static/shard.js`, `static/art/`; the reviewer's fix round of the same day is in this entry). **Which pictures.** `itemicon.icon_name` is the rule of 2026-11-11, and then, for an item that fits the main hand, the off hand or the range and that the rule gave no picture, the head word of its name says one of thirteen (`HELD_WORDS`): an idol (totem, idol, doll, statue, statuette, figurine, effigy, fetish), a stone (stone, gem, crystal, globe, pearl, shard, rock, jewel), a horn (horn, and a horn mouthpiece), a lantern (torch, lantern, candle, lamp, brazier), a cup (stein, mug, chalice, cup, goblet, flask, vial, bottle, flagon, tankard), a scroll (scroll, writ, parchment, note, letter, map), a rod (rod, baton, cane, and a scepter or sceptre), a whip (whip, lash, scourge), a claw (claw, talon), a pick (pick, pickaxe, mattock), a polearm (halberd, scythe, naginata, voulge, glaive, poleaxe, polearm, bardiche: a poleaxe was an axe) and a charm (brooch, talisman, charm, amulet, medal, medallion, symbol, sigil, emblem, token, badge); the two words `fishing pole` and `fishing rod` (and the one word fishingpole) are a fishing pole and beat the rod, a pole alone is nothing. **The review took five guesses out**: a stick, a bo and a bokken are no rods, an eye is no stone (an eyeball is not a crystal ball) and a seal is no charm; a `bo stick` and a `walking stick` are staves (phrases), and so is a `bo` that is a two-handed blunt weapon; every other stick, a bokken, a tanto bo and a wakizashi bo have no picture of their own (the pouch on a held row). **They are for the held only**: a ring named `... Seal`, an earring named `... Stone` and a neck piece named `Talisman` are worn and keep exactly what they showed (tested for every word on every worn place). **Nothing that had a picture loses it**: the new words are looked at only where the rule gave none (an orb keeps the orb, a club and a scepter that its skill makes a mace keep the mace, a stone that is thrown keeps the thrown picture, a shield the catalog says is a shield keeps the shield), with the one exception that is the maintainer's: an instrument whose head word is horn is the horn (5 items), and every other instrument keeps the instrument. **A word is narrowed where it would be a guess**: `mouthpiece` is a horn only as `horn mouthpiece` (a flute's mouthpiece is nothing), and the words that the scan of the release table found and the lists do not name stay without a picture of their own, so that the pouch beats a wrong picture: ball (a crystal ball), lightstone and the other -stone compounds, bowl, deity, lariat, partisan, ahlspiess, crook, shovel, sprinkler, broom, censer, jutte, tonfa, kusarigama, crossbow, buckler and webshield, manual, sapphire and the rest of the 183 (the lists are in the report of the branch). Of the 483 held items of the release table that had no picture of their own, 300 now have one (293 of the thirteen and 7 staves) and 183 have none (178 of them fit held places only); 3,106 of the 6,820 items have a picture of their own (2,806 before), among them 40 rods, 27 stones and 10 charms (every one is listed in the report). **The pouch is for an item that fits a held place and has no picture of its own, and for no other.** In the main hand, the off hand and the range (`art.row_icon`: only what fits them stands there) a row is the item's own picture, else the pouch, and no sword and no bow stands in. **An any row** takes whatever fits, so it shows what the item is by its places (`art.item_picture`): its own picture, else the pouch if it fits a held place, else what the page heading of the item shows (the place's picture when every place it fits is worn and they have the one picture, none otherwise); the first version gave every filled held row the pouch, and 621 any rows of the default kits (a robe, a cloak, a girdle: worn-only items) showed one. An empty held row is as it was (none) and a worn row is as it was. **The page of an item** has its own picture, else the pouch if it fits held places only (178 items), else the place's as before; an item of both held and worn places and no picture of its own has none on its page, and the pouch in its held rows. **A picture of the held things stays on a held row**: the thirteen and the pouch (`art.HELD_ONLY_ICONS`) are never drawn on a worn row, where an item of both places (Tsiraka's Claws, Idol of Woven Grass, Pulsating Gem, Gem of Rage, Cinctured Whip, which have a picture of their own) has the place's picture, as at 8f9be10; its held rows, its any row and its page heading have the picture. It is the mechanism that keeps a picture of armor in the place of its own part, for these pictures. **Where the data is.** `ic` (a shard's and the list's) is the picture an item has by its places (`art.item_picture`: its own, else the pouch, else its heading's), and `pl` the picture a filled row of a place shows for an item with none of its own (a worn place's own, the pouch for the range, the main hand and the off hand, none for an any row, whose item decides); no field was added. The browser (`rowIcon`) draws a worn row from the item's `ic` and the place (a picture of the held things gives the place's, armor as before), a row of the range, the main hand or the off hand from `ic`, else the pouch, an any row from `ic` alone (so an item that the file does not know has none in an any row, where a held place shows the pouch: without the list of the site, a held row of an answer shows the pouch and an any row none), and an empty held row none; the number of the first held place (`HELD_FROM = 17`) and the names of the pictures that stay on a held row (`HELD_PICTURE`) are held to the generator by tests. **The characters of a data file.** The list of the item table names every picture the site can draw, which is now 69 (the 62 characters of a digit, a small letter and a capital were not enough), so `data.ICON_DIGITS` (and its copy in `shard.js`, held to it by a test; the two end-to-end and unit tests read the string from the file) was rebuilt: the ten digits, the consonants in both cases (**no vowel and no y**, so that a string of codes cannot spell a word: the privacy scan of a built site flagged a run of letters of an `ic` string that equalled a term it holds, which a mostly-dash string never did), 22 plain ASCII punctuation characters and the backtick, 73 in all, so 4 more than the 69 pictures the list names: none that a JSON string, a page or a quote would change (one byte each), and no colon, slash or backslash, since a string of the characters of a file could then read as a path to the release scan; a file that names more is still a `BuildError` (`check_icons`). **The art.** The 14 pictures (the 13 and the pouch, `icon-<name>.png` of the artist's folder, 32x32, 12 to 61 colours each) are cells of the one sheet, after the 55 that were there (no cell moved): the sheet is 272x306, 21,276 bytes (17,483 before). The cut to a palette is the tool's, over 2,208 colours: the worst channel is 16 of 255 where it was 10 (the mean CIE76 distance is 1.2, as it was 1.4), so the manifest's `tolerance` is 16 (it was 12; the test's ceiling, 16, is the old one), and the `palette` is 254: the palette of 255 had the two entries (47,47,49) and (47,122,197) side by side, whose bytes `//1/` the release scan reads as a share path (it reads every chunk of a PNG but the picture), and 254 does not; the next time a cell is added the release check will say if it happens again. **Budgets.** Art 211,427 to 215,220 bytes: `art.BUDGET_BYTES` 210 KB to 215 KB (220,160, 4,940 spare; 210 KB was 180 bytes short); the largest trio page 67,092 of 67,584 (492 spare, was 550: the limit is not raised); the largest shard 196,309 of 204,800 (196,271 before: the characters of `ic` carry more, the length is the same); the list of the pictures 8,295 bytes raw and 4,748 gzipped of 16 KB and 6 KB; every other budget as it was. Edits to existing tests, on purpose: the tests that said a held row shows none (`test_site_art.py`, `test_site_icons.py`, `tests-js/item-icons.test.mjs`, `tests-e2e/item-icons.spec.mjs`), the tests of the denied words (they now say which words are still denied and which have a picture) and of the list of the pictures the rule can give, the 62 characters, the art budget (`test_art_process.py`, `test_site_build.py`) and the floor of the pictures the release check reads (the sheet's cells), and the contract fixtures (the Any slot 1 row of the trio page, and `ic`, `pl` and `icons` of the four shards).
- 2026-11-16 Layout polish for a reader who arrives from a link (branch layout-polish; `static/site.css`, `static/trio.js`, `static/picker.js`, `templates/base.html.j2`, `home.html.j2`, `trio.html.j2`, `site/build.py`, `site/content.py`, `static/art/manifest.json` (the `use` notes of three scenes); presentation only: no datum, no solver output, no address and no saved state changed). **Headers.** `thead th` has `overflow-wrap: normal`: the cause of "ARRIVE / S AT" was the rule that lets a cell break anywhere (`th, td`), which makes a column's least width one letter; a header now breaks between its words and never inside one, so the column is as wide as its longest word, at every width (the clipped headers of a phone's cards too). Row headers (a long item name) still break anywhere. **The Stats button** is in the middle of its row where a row is a row (`top: calc(50% - 0.775rem)` from 37.51rem up, half of its 1.55rem; the cells are centred, so it stands level with the tier and the source and never depends on what else the row holds: a name of two lines, the notes of Exalt, a chip of haste); a phone's cards are as they were (the button at the right of the name's first line). The rows the browser draws are the rows the page is built with, so nothing differs between them; the test measures both. **The settings block.** Race, Role and Tier are one row at 1280 px with the widest set of roles (the selects are 15 and 9.5rem wide at 37.51rem and up, the role buttons have a narrower padding, the gap is 1rem); a narrower window wraps them and a phone stacks them, as before. The assumptions are one `details#assumes` (closed), whose summary is "Assumptions: <weapons>, <buffs>[, <weights>]" (the words of a phone's one line, which are made together with it), and whose body is today's three assumptions, each a details element with its fieldset and its ids as they were, one under another, and the AA line; the old heading and the word "Assumes" are gone. **A choice that is not the default is said without opening it**: the first word of the summary is "Assumptions changed" (and `data-changed` is on the element, which the stylesheet marks with a bar that moves nothing) when the buffs are not the page's, a weapon setup is not the role's best, or a slider is not at x1; the race and the tier are no assumption. It is a native details element: the keyboard (Enter, Space), a screen reader (an expanded state, the summary as its name) and the tap target (2.75rem) are those of the others on the page; the one new stop of the Tab order is its summary, between the link of the role's hint and the first button of the answer. **The key to the markers.** The two markers whose words say least, Developer and Model, say what they mean in a `title` (`content.SEALS_TO_EXPLAIN`, the words of `content.SEALS` that the rules page prints: the same text, taken, never written again) and the trio page has one line, `#seal-key`, in the section of the answer between the kit and the rules of its tiers: "Seals say how far to trust a rule: Developer: a developer statement or patch note; Model: our or EQEmu's formula, or a judgment constant", and a link to all five on the rules page. The sidebar's last line has the same title on its marker (it is on every page). **Why under the kit and why two titles, not five:** the first row of the kit is, on the test's 360 x 740 phone, 7 px inside the first screen, so a line above the kit would take the answer off it; under the kit is where a phone's first markers are (above it, the folded switches hide the hint's). Titles on all five would have added about 760 bytes to the largest page, which has 574 spare (220 in the build of a release), and a raise of the limit was not needed for two; the two named have the words that say least, and the other three (a file of the client, a measurement, what players report) say what they are. The markers lost `focusable="false"` (the browsers that need it are not supported), the span round their word and the class `icon` (`.seal > svg`), which paid for the key and the titles. **The home page.** The first sentence says what a trio is ("A trio is the three classes one character combines in EverQuest Legends."); the rest of the intro is as it was. The archway (`hero-trio`) is the one picture of the page, at its own size (1x, 206 x 142 px with its frame, no caption: a caption would say Pick three a fourth time), beside the words from 20rem + its own width up and under them on a phone, where it is **now shown** (it was `display: none` on a phone so that it was never fetched; it is 19 KB of the phone's 120 KB, which `art-phone.spec.mjs` holds). The plains and the dragon are **not on the page** (a strip of 120 px would have been a crop of art made to be seen whole): their files and manifest entries stay, `plains.png` and `dragon.png` are drawn by no page now, and their `use` notes say so. **Pick three classes is said once:** in the heading of the picker; the group label is `sr-only` (still the name of the group); the status line is empty in the page and gives an empty line no room (`#picker-status:empty` is the visually hidden style, which keeps it in the page, a live region that must exist before its first message) and says something only when a class is chosen or a word is no class; the script no longer writes "Pick three classes." and does not write an empty text node. **The footer.** The links are one `ul.foot-links` (the report link or the line that says when reports open, About, Changelog): a flex row that wraps between its links, with no separator, so none can stand at the start or the end of a line, with or without the report link (the build without an owner has none); the four badges are a grid of two columns at every width (the plates are narrower than a phone's half of the line, so the words of a badge wrap inside the plate on a phone), and the third, "Made with Notepad", a label with no link, is "Latest changes", an `a.badge` of the same plate (`badge-scroll`) that leads to the changelog (the other badges are CSS plates with words, not pictures: no new file). **Budgets** (from a real build of the results of data-2026-10-27, `--noindex`): **the largest trio page** (rng-mnk-shm) is 67,092 bytes before and 67,010 after in the build the brief names (`--noindex`, no owner), and 67,446 before and 67,364 after in the indexable build (`--owner`) that a release is, of the 67,584 that 66 KB is: 574 and 220 spare, where it was 492 and 138, **so the limit is not raised** (the key, the titles and the folded line are paid for by the three things the markers lost, and by the section's heading and the word Assumes); the largest page gzipped is 12.7 to 12.8 KB (12.9 to 13.0 KB indexable), the first load of a trio page 38.6 to 38.9 KB of 60 (38.7 to 39.1 indexable); **first-load JS** 16.1 to 16.2 KB gzip of 30 (46.4 to 46.7 KB raw of 100; the stylesheet 10.0 to 10.2 KB gzip, the page script 0.3 KB longer: the folded line's words); **the home page** 66,872 to 66,668 bytes (65.3 to 65.1 KB of 80, 9.0 to 8.9 KB gzip of 12; indexable 67,116 to 66,912), first load 35.1 to 35.3 KB of 60; **the 404 page** 12,172 to 12,214 bytes (11.9 KB of 16 both, 3.9 KB gzip of 5 both), first load 15.8 to 16.0 KB; **the largest item page** 13,330 to 13,152 bytes (3.8 KB gzip of 4 both; indexable 4,021 to 4,033 bytes gzipped of 4,096, so 63 spare where it was 75: the titles of the markers on a page of an item); **the art** is the same 28 files, 215,220 of 220,160 bytes (no art added, none removed, none changed); every other budget is as it was (the shards, the runner-ups, the effects, the item table, the optimizer, the files: 9,443 of 15,000).  **Edits to existing tests, on purpose:** `test_site_build.py` (the AA line is in the folded `details#assumes`, whose summary has the lead; the footer's third badge is "Latest changes" and leads to the changelog; the panel of the switches holds a details element for the assumptions, not a section), `test_site_home.py` (the status line of the picker is empty in the page), `test_site_setpieces.py` (the home page's scenes are the archway alone; its pictures have no captions; the phone's rules no longer hide the archway and the plains, which are not on the page; the home page has the archway beside its words), `tests-js/picker.test.mjs` (three assertions: no class chosen says nothing, where it said "Pick three classes."), `tests-e2e/home.spec.mjs` (the same two places), `art-phone.spec.mjs` (the archway is fetched by a phone, and no page fetches the plains or the dragon), `support.mjs` (`openSwitches` opens the folded line of the assumptions as well unless it is told not to, and `openAssumptions` opens only that), `trio.spec.mjs` (the order of the keyboard's stops has the folded line after the three choices; the keyboard tests of the race, the role, the tier and the weapons of a role that lacks a setup leave the line closed until they need it; the four tests that clicked an assumption's line first open the folded line), `common-items.spec.mjs`, `weights.spec.mjs` and `upgrade.spec.mjs` (the same: the assumptions are behind the folded line), and the contract copies `fixtures/site/contract/home-main.html` and `trio-main.html` and `fixtures/site/contract-exalt/trio-main.html` (made again by the generator). No test was weakened, skipped or deleted; the tests that said what the page was now say what it is. New: `test_site_layout.py`, `tests-js/assumptions-line.test.mjs`, `tests-e2e/layout.spec.mjs` (measured with the boxes the browser gives: no header breaks inside a word at 360, 768 and 1280 px, the Stats buttons are one column level with their rows and the names one left edge in the built and the drawn rows, Race, Role and Tier are one row, the folded line opens from the keyboard and says a change, the picker is on the first screen, the footer row and the badges are even at four widths, and nothing this change made moves when the code starts).
- 2026-11-16 Layout polish, the fix round of the review (branch layout-polish; `static/site.css`, `templates/trio.html.j2`, `templates/home.html.j2`; it amends the entry above about the Stats button, the key and the intro). **All six points were real** (the sixth in part: the plain scan of the trio page covers the closed line on a desktop, nothing covered it with a phone's switches open or with "Assumptions changed"). **The Stats button moved when its panel opened** because it was centred in its row (`top: calc(50% - 0.775rem)`) and the open panel grows the row downwards (36 and 44 px at 1280 and 768 on the real clr-shd-shm page). CSS alone cannot know the height the row had when it was closed, so the button is not centred any more: from 37.51rem up the cells of a row begin at its top (`vertical-align: top`), a cell has `--kit-first` above its first line (the cell's padding, 0.55rem, and half of what the 32 px picture of the place, times `--kit-s`, 1 or 2 from 56rem, times `--art-m`, is taller than a line of 1.4725rem), so that the first line of every cell is level with the middle of the picture of the place, and the button is pinned to that line (`top: calc(var(--kit-first) - 0.03875rem)`). A row of one line looks as it did (its line is in the middle of the picture); a tall row has its words along the top, where they were centred, and nothing in a row moves when a panel opens, the first line of the tier and the source included. The tests measure the box of the button before and after it opens and after it closes, on a tall row (notes under the name) and a short one, at 768 and 1280, and that its middle is the middle of its name's first line and of the picture. **The button met the words** (1.3 to 2.6 px: "Sergeant's Leather Belt" at 360 and 768, "Handmaiden's Wristwrap" at 1280): the room kept for it, 4.6rem, was less than its width and its inset together (4.81rem). It is 5.4rem now from 37.51rem up (width, inset, a gap of 0.59rem); on a phone, where the button is at the card's edge (inset 0, the card has a padding of its own) and the first row of the test's phone has no pixel to spare, 4.8rem (a gap of 0.49rem): 5.4rem there wrapped the first name and put the first row 26 px below the first screen. A test sets names of 8 to 70 characters in every row, with a chip of haste, closed and with every panel open, at 360, 768 and 1280, on the fixture and on the preview with the Exalt notes (a row with a very long name is in the fixture), and tests the box of every line of the item cell against the button's. On the real page, before the round: one clash at each of 360, 768 and 1280 px (2.6, 1.3 and 2.1 px) and the waist button 44 and 36 px lower with its panel open at 768 and 1280; after: no clash and no move. **The key** is above the kit on a wide window: the first thing of the section of the answer after its title bar and the button All stats, and above the table, with `max-width: none` and one line at 1280 px (its first word is "Seals:" now: "Seals say how far to trust a rule:" made two lines, and the paragraph's 80ch is lifted for it). **It is under the kit on a phone**, as before, because the first row of the kit is 7 px inside the first screen of the test's phone: at 37.5rem and below the section of the answer is a flex column and the key has `order: 1` after the table (the paragraphs of rules `order: 2`), so the markup, what a screen reader reads and the order of the Tab key (the link to all the seals) have the key before the kit everywhere, and only the picture of a phone has it after; a flex container keeps the margin of its first child inside, so the heading has none there and the section has the 1rem that the heading had, which collapses with what is above it as it did.
