The page is not bigger (the markup moved, and "Seals:" is 29 bytes shorter than before). **The intro** names the game once ("exact answers, with the evidence and the confidence behind every rule": nothing else was lost). **Axe**: a scan with the switches open and the line closed, and one with the line closed and saying "Assumptions changed", in the four views. The check that no page but a trio page has the key looked at the text of a node, which never holds an id: it looks for the element now. Budgets after, from real builds (noindex; indexable): the largest trio page 67,092 bytes at the start of the branch, 67,010 after the first round and 66,982 now in the build the brief names (`--noindex`, no owner; 602 spare of 67,584) and 67,446, 67,364 and 67,336 in the indexable build (`--owner`) that a release is (248 spare, it had 138); first load of a trio page 39.0 KB (39.2 indexable) of 60; first-load JS 16.2 KB gzip of 30 (46.7 KB raw of 100), the stylesheet 10.3 KB gzip; the home page 66,646 bytes (66,890 indexable); the largest item page 13,152 bytes and, in the indexable build, 4,033 of 4,096 gzipped (as before the round); the art 215,220 of 220,160 bytes, 28 files, unchanged; no limit raised. Edits to tests that were there before the round: none outside the two files of this branch (`test_site_layout.py`, `layout.spec.mjs`: the Stats test says level with the name where it said the middle of the row, the key's tests say above the kit and under it on a phone, the intro's say the sentence) and the three contract copies, made again by the generator (`fixtures/site/contract/home-main.html`, `contract/trio-main.html`, `contract-exalt/trio-main.html`).
- 2026-11-16 Where an exalt donor comes from (branch exalt-source; `site/build.py`, `site/data.py`, `static/ui/exalt.js`; a table of texts in the effects file the page already fetches and the way the section draws it: no rule, no solver output, no address, no saved state and no data source changed, nothing in the page of a trio, nothing in the optimizer or the evaluator of a shared kit). **What the reader gets.** An Exalt line under a place of Wear this said "from Black Alloy Medallion (Drop)"; the word in the parentheses is now a button where the site knows where the donor comes from, and it opens "Source: Plane of Hate (Innoruuk)", beneath the line, with a link to the item's page (which the line does not otherwise have). In the section's plan, its list of donors to farm and its ranked lists the same text is in plain view beside each donor ("Drop · Plane of Hate (Innoruuk)"), with no click. A donor with no known source says what it said. **The text** is the one the Source column of a kit row says, made by the one function that words it (`precompute.shards.source_summary`: the first live drop with its creature, else a live quest, vendor, recipe or foraging, else the first of each; at most 60 characters), so the same item is worded the same everywhere and there is no second rule. The Source column shows one place (the first by that function's order), so the opened text shows that one: the item's page lists all of them, which is why the opened text links to it. **Where it is made, and why not in the effects layer.** In the site build, from the catalog the build already reads for the pictures of the armor and holds to the results by the fingerprint of the item table (`build.read_catalog`, split from `load_icon_extras`, which keeps its behaviour and its messages; `build.donor_sources`). The effects layer's writer (`effects/files.py`) is one of the files of the release's code hash (`assemble.WRITER_FILES`), so a field written there would make every release already built stale, and results already made would show nothing; written in the build, the existing release shows the texts with no new run. A build without the catalog (`--no-item-catalog`: the synthetic fixture's, never a release's) writes no texts and its warning says so; a donor whose catalog item has no sources in the catalog's form stops the build with one line that names it. **The file.** `src` in `data/effects.json`, after `items`: an item key to its text, one entry for each donor that has a text, none for one that has none (the positional entry of `items`, six fields, is as it was: the matcher and two tests read it). No new request: the file is the one the page already fetches once it has shown. `data.check_donor_sources` holds the table (a mapping of donor keys to texts that are not empty, at most `DONOR_SOURCE_LIMIT` = 60 characters, with no control character; a test holds the limit equal to the Source column's `SOURCE_LIMIT`), and the page ignores an entry that is not such a text and a table that is not a table. **The form** is a native button with the disclosure pattern (`aria-expanded`, `aria-controls`, a name that starts with the word it shows, "Where Crown of Leaves comes from: Drop", a target of at least 24 px) and not a tooltip (a phone has no hover, and the name of the effect beside it is already a tooltip: two hover texts in one line would be a trap) and not a `details` element (its summary is a block and cannot stand inside the parentheses of the sentence). The text is a block under the line in the item cell, so it grows the row and nothing else: the Stats button is pinned to the first line of the cell and the cell keeps its room for it, and the layout test measures the button before and after a text opens and closes, in every row that has one, with a text of 60 characters in words and in one word, at 360, 768 and 1280 px (no move, no word meets it). Everything is set as text. **Budgets** (real builds of the results of data-2026-10-27, `--noindex` and indexable with `--owner`): the shared effects file 93,456 bytes raw and 16,727 gzipped before, 110,053 and 21,247 after, of its 200 KB and 50 KB (371 donors, every one with a text, 15.5 KB raw and 4.7 KB gzipped of it; no limit raised and none needed); the largest trio page (rng-mnk-shm) 66,982 bytes before and after in the noindex build and 67,336 before and after in the indexable one (248 of 67,584 spare, as it was: the limit is not raised); first-load JS 16.2 KB gzip of 30 before and after (46.7 KB raw of 100); first load of a trio page 39.0 KB (39.2 indexable) of 60 before and after; the evaluator of a shared kit 77,394 bytes raw (75.6 KB of 80) and 26,115 gzipped (25.5 KB of 28), the same file; the chunk of the section (`exalt-*.js`, loaded when the page is idle) 26,545 bytes raw and 10,718 gzipped before, 28,463 and 11,203 after, so the chunks loaded on first use are 152.2 KB of 250 where they were 150.3. **Tests.** New: `pipeline/tests/test_site_exalt_source.py` (every donor's text is exactly the Source column's, one donor of each kind of source and one with none, the live second drop first, the file's shape and budgets, a build without the catalog, a catalog item without sources, the reader's checks, every item of the committed catalog), `tests-js/exalt-source.test.mjs`, `tests-e2e/exalt-source.spec.mjs` (keyboard, the accessible name, the target, axe with a text open in both themes, the plan in plain view, the kit of another tier) with `tests-e2e/exalt-source-support.mjs` (the fixture is built without the catalog, so a route gives its donors texts on the way to the page and the page is held to the file it got), and a test in `tests-e2e/layout.spec.mjs` for the Stats button. No existing test was edited, weakened, skipped or deleted.
- 2026-11-16 Where an exalt donor comes from, the fix round (branch exalt-source; `precompute/shards.py`, `site/build.py`, `site/data.py`, `static/ui/exalt.js`; it amends the entry above where it says the table holds one text for a donor, the label of the opened text and the name of the button). **Checked in the code first: five of the six points were real; the sixth is left as it is.** **1. The word and the text disagreed.** The word of a line is `effects/donors.py: access_cost` (the cheapest route: vendor, then quest or recipe, then drop), the text was `source_summary` (a drop in a live zone first): 46 of the 371 donors of the release said "Quest or crafted" and opened a drop (Staff of Elemental Mastery: Earth, White Gold Necklace, Ghoulbane), and 81 have more than one source, of which one was shown. **`src` is now, for each donor, the list of all its sources**, each worded as the Source column words one source, the kind the word names first (`build.WORD_KINDS`: 1 vendor; 2 quest or recipe; 3 and 4 drop; a test holds it equal to `access_cost`), then the others each in the Source column's order, no text twice, at most `DONOR_SOURCES_MOST` = 4; the text the Source column says is always one of them (if it did not fit it takes the 4th place), and after the texts a whole number of at least 1 is the count of the sources that did not fit (`["a", "b", 3]`), the count and not the texts, since the item's page lists them. In the real release: 290 donors have one source, 58 two, 11 three, 3 four, 7 five, 1 six and 1 eight (487 texts, 9 donors with a count); 0 donors disagree with their word and 0 miss the Source column's text. **The wording of one source is shared**: `precompute/shards.py` has small functions for one drop, vendor, recipe or foraged zone, `source_texts(item, live)` (every source, `(kind, text)`, in the column's own order, each cut to 60 characters) and `source_summary` is its first, so it is one rule. **What this costs, said plainly:** `shards.py` is a file of the release's code hash (`assemble.WRITER_FILES`), so the hash of the writers changes and the deploy check sees a release already built as made by other writers; the files are written again from the chunks at the next release build (no solve). What `source_summary` says is unchanged: a test holds the SHA-256 of its output over the 11,380 items of the committed catalog, for four sets of live sources, made with the function as it was. **2. The build holds the texts to the shards.** In `build()`, every donor that is also an item with a Source column in a shard or a runner-up table of any trio must have exactly that text among its texts (`build.check_source_column`; a miss, or a donor with none, is a `BuildError` that names the item and the text); both ways are tested, in a shard and in a runner-up table. **3.** `site_effects_json`'s `ValueError` was outside the `except` that makes the others a `BuildError`: it is one line now, tested. **4.** The name of the button starts with the word it shows ("Drop: where Crown of Leaves comes from"; the claim of the entry above is true now). **5. Looks.** The bracket, the word and the bracket are one piece (`.exalt-where`, `white-space: nowrap`: the line wraps before it, never inside it), "Item page" and "+N more" do not break in two, and the opened block is a group named by the donor ("Where Crown of Leaves comes from") with a bar at its left edge that ties it to its line and the label "Where:" (on a phone it sat right above the row's own "Source" of another item, under the old label). The texts are one to a line, then the note "and N more on the item page", then the link. In the section's plan, the list of donors to farm and the ranked lists the first text is in plain view and "+N more" is a link to the item's page (a plain word where the donor has no page); the browser tests measure that the bracket piece and the links are on one line at 360 and 1280 px. **6. Left as it is:** when a kit is drawn again (a tier, a race, Equip now) an open text may close, and a button that had the focus loses it to the page, as the neighbouring "In the plan" link does: nothing in `clearHints` hands the focus on today, and no state was added for it. **Budgets** (real builds of the results of data-2026-10-27, `--noindex` and indexable): the shared effects file 110,053 raw and 21,247 gzipped after the first round, 114,337 and 22,178 now (`src` alone 19.8 KB and 5.8 KB), of 200 KB and 50 KB, unchanged limits; the largest trio page 66,982 (indexable 67,336, 248 spare) as at the start; first-load JS 16.2 KB gzip of 30; first load of a trio page 39.0 (39.2) KB of 60; the evaluator of a shared kit the same file (75.6 KB of 80, 25.5 KB gzip of 28); the chunk of the section 29,733 bytes raw and 11,583 gzipped (it was 28,463 and 11,203), the chunks loaded on first use 153.4 KB of 250. **Tests** (new, shown failing first): `test_site_exalt_source.py` is held to the new form (58 tests: the first text of every donor is of the kind its word names, the Source column is among them, the count, the checks against the shards, the validator of lists, the golden digests of `source_summary`, the committed catalog), `tests-js/exalt-source.test.mjs`, `tests-e2e/exalt-source.spec.mjs` and the layout test. No existing test was edited.
- 2026-11-18 Kit checks (`python -m trioforge.checks.kits`; the how-to is in CONTRIBUTING, "Checking the kits"): a scoreboard, not a ranking of players' opinions and not a set of targets, of properties of the kits that a player would call obviously right. It reads `items.json` and the shards of a results folder and imports the model and the rules but no solver code, so it can be run before and after a change of the solver. A **kit** is one view of a trio: the default race of `defaults.json`, the best weapon setup of that race, role and tier (the highest `F`, a tie to the first setup the shard lists), the default evidence level (`wiki` while the strict pool is empty, as in a release) and the own buffs: one kit for each trio, role and tier, and no count mixes other views. A kit that passes every check is not therefore the best kit; the numbers say how often a property holds and whether a change moved them. Do not commit the scoreboard of a build that has the private seen flags merged: its counts are the flags in another form. **A gate is refused when the results cannot be trusted** (exit 2, one line on stderr): no kit was read, a trio gave none (its default race has no scenario), the results were made with other rules than the checkout's (`rules_version` of `items.json`); without `--gate` the same facts are `warning:` lines and a `warnings` list in the JSON. A shard that lacks a total, an item the table lacks, an item or a race the model does not know are errors with exit 2, never a traceback.
- 2026-11-18 K1 is a scoreboard and has no threshold (round 2, which replaces the first line of this entry, a limit of a quarter on the share of WIS, INT and mana in a melee or tank kit): the design review found that stamina, not mana, drives the kits (in the named melee and tank kits of the first baseline K10 puts one point of STA at 5.6 to 7.8 HP-equivalents and one of WIS or INT at 0 to 5.6, 2.8 for most; in their caster kits a point of WIS or INT is worth up to 45), so a share of caster stats is no defect in itself. It is kept as two numbers, as distributions: `K1.attr`, the share of WIS and INT among the attribute points (STR STA AGI DEX WIS INT CHA) of the kit's items, and `K1.pool`, the share of the stat points that count for the objective that go to the mana pool (WIS, INT and mana; the stats that the weights of the role value, WIS and INT only where a class of the trio makes its mana of it, and mana only where some class has a pool; haste, regen and resists are not stat points). The second is the number K1 was; its description no longer says "caster stats". K1 and K6 are not targets.
- 2026-11-18 K3 0.5: in a tank kit of a trio with a class of the heaviest armour, the AC of each of the chest, legs, arms, head, hands and feet must be at least half of the best AC that the trio, the race, the pool and the tier allow in that slot. Round 2 makes every armour check **independent of the placement**: the two Any places take any item and a swap of two items between a named place and an Any place leaves the score of the kit as it was, so a piece counts for its slot worn in the named place or in an Any place (the best AC among them), and the best AC the trio can wear leaves out a piece whose stats besides AC are only WIS, INT, CHA, mana or mana regeneration (the K2 definition with the AC left aside: armour a tank would not count) and, as the solver does, an item marked not-for-bis (`ItemTable.pool`). A kit passes `K3` when no slot fails. `K3.robe`, the second gate, fails a tank kit whose best chest piece (the most AC at the tier, a tie to the piece that is no robe) is a robe, wherever it is worn; a kit with no chest piece has no robe (its chest fails K3 instead). `K3.robe_worn`, scoreboard only, counts the kits that wear a robe anywhere. The threshold: the same baseline at +4 shows the best piece worn where the objective takes it (hands 1, arms 1, as medians) and not elsewhere, so half is a line that the slots that can pass do pass.
- 2026-11-18 Armour classes and robes: the catalog has no armour type, so the heaviest armour is read from the class lists of the **pool** (the items a solve pool holds: level strict or wiki, no deity lock, not not-for-bis; a hidden or disputed item is worn by no kit). A list of three classes or more that at least three items of the pool hold in each of the six armour slots is a generic set (every class counts as the list of all; with the pool alone, ten items a slot, which counted the hidden ones, is too many, and 2 to 4 give the same answer); the reach of a class is the number of generic sets it is in; the heavy classes are those of the greatest reach (here the five of plate: Warrior, Cleric, Paladin, Shadow Knight and Bard), the cloth classes those outside the largest generic set that is not all classes (the four cloth casters). A **robe** is a piece of casters: it lists a cloth class, every class it lists is a caster class of the model (`model.roles.CASTER_CLASSES`) and it lists no heavy class. The first rule (a cloth class and no heavy class) called a chest piece that a Monk and a Rogue also wear a robe; the new rule does not, and it also drops a piece that is named a robe and that a Monk wears. The names of the items are not read.
- 2026-11-18 The other definitions of the kit checks. `K2`: a pure caster item is one whose only positive stats (after the tier rule) are WIS, INT, CHA, mana or mana regeneration, with at least one of them; a weapon (its damage is a stat), an item with worn haste and an item whose only stats are resists are not; the report gives the number of distinct items behind the failures and the five worn most (one face piece is in 220 of the 221 failing kits of the first baseline). `K4`: a set is an item-name stem (a name with `of` takes what follows the last `of`, any other name all but its last word) that at least three items of the pool carry in at least four different slot groups; a kit wears a set when it has two different pieces or more of it. `K5`: a bow is an item of the Archery skill worn in the Range slot or an Any place, and the check is for the roles where a bow can be scored (balanced, melee, and the `ranged` role of another branch), not a tank and not a caster; the solver does not score Range damage today. `K6`: the strength of the naked character (race and class modifiers) plus the gear's, each under the stat cap (`stats.hard_cap`), against the most the pool gives (a best set of items for the 22 places, an item that is not Lore worn in every place that takes it and a Lore item once, as the solver wears them; the Any places open to every item; the hands not held to a setup), so the share is an upper bound's. `K7`: the five resists as the sheet counts them. `K8`: the sheet AC against `caps.sheet_softcap` of the shard. Buffs are left out of K6 (the shard does not list their strength).
- 2026-11-18 K10, exchange rates (scoreboard only, `checks/exchange.py`): for a kit, the change of the objective for one more point of each of STA STR AGI DEX WIS INT CHA AC HP MANA, of each resist school the model scores, of worn haste and of ATK, by `formulas.evaluate_truthful` under the profile of the trio, race and role (`make_profile`), the own buffs the shard names for the kit and its weapon setup: read-only use of the model, nothing solved, nothing approximated. A point of a stat or a resist goes to the first worn item (a test holds that the item does not matter), a point of haste to the worn item with the most haste. A kit whose shard value is not the evaluator's value to the two decimals a shard keeps is refused with both numbers (the run stops, exit 2): the results were not made by this evaluator. It is printed for the named cases (K9) and written in the JSON; every named kit of the first baseline agrees with the evaluator. `K11` (scoreboard only): the stamina total (race and class, the buffs the shard names, the gear) is within 2 points under `stats.knee` (253 or 254 for a knee of 255; at the knee or past it does not count), the place where the solver parks stamina; a count by role.
- 2026-11-18 The gates of the kit checks are `K2` and the robe part of `K3`; both fail today, by design, so `--gate` is in neither the release build nor CI. Once a change of the solver makes them pass on a full set of results, the command can be added after the release checks of the build and to CI with `--gate`, and a failure of either then stops a release.
- 2026-11-18 The Bow weapon setup and the Ranged role (branch ranged-role; `rules/rules.json`, the model, the solver, the precompute, the exporter, the browser engine, the site and the parity fixtures changed together). **What.** A new weapon setup `ranged`, labelled "Bow": the range place holds a bow (a weapon of the archery skill that fits the range place, `weapons.is_bow`; arrows have no delay and are no weapon, a thrown weapon has another skill) and the bow is scored by the measure a main hand is scored by, `(damage + bonus) / delay x (1 + haste / 100)` at the haste the kit counts, the bonus the listed one else half the delay, times the new rule `weapons.ranged_factor`; the main hand and the off hand are the stat stick's (anything, chosen for its stats, no damage scored, a two-hander never in the off hand), and in every other setup the range place scores nothing and takes what it always took. A new role `ranged` ("Ranged"), offered to a trio with a Ranger whatever else it has (a trio of three melee classes with a Ranger has Balanced and Ranged), after the other roles; its only setup is the bow; Balanced does not average its template (the other roles are not re-weighted). **Offered exactly when.** The setup is the last of the setups of Balanced, Tank and Melee, and the only one of Ranged, at an evidence level when the solve pool of that level (the strict items, and for the wiki level the wiki items too; no deity's item) holds a bow that a class of the trio may equip (`weapons.bow_available`, read from the class list of each bow, as the shield setup is read from the shields: no list of classes is in the code); with no pool to read there is none (a shield is assumed with no pool, a bow is not), so the profile of a caller that gives no pool, and the fixtures that make one so, are as they were; the Caster role never has it; the Ranged role plays the stat stick at a level whose pool has no bow, as a role with nothing else does. The weapon rules read the range place as a hand: `allowed_in_hand(item, "range", setup)` and `damage_index_exact(item, "range", ...)`, `formulas.weapon_index` and `evaluate` read the RANGE place, the builder, the pruning, `check_kit`, the runner-ups and the Upgrade functions ask the same function for the group RANGE, and the canonical placement of a solve arranges the range place with the hands under the bow setup (a spare bow worn in an Any place scores no damage, so which bow stands in the range place is part of the value; the engine's port does the same). The range place is not forced to be filled: a bow is the only thing that may stand there in the setup and the objective decides, as it decides the hands of the other setups. Arrows: a kit has no ammunition place and none was added; the page says in the note of the bow setup that arrow damage is not counted. **New rules (each a judgment: seal model, source "TrioForge judgment", status active).** `weapons.ranged_factor` = 1.0, a bow shot counts as much as a main hand swing: nothing in the evidence measures it, so the bow starts level with a main hand and the rule says so; it is held in every profile (`Profile.ranged_factor`, `EvalProfile.ranged_factor`, `ranged_factor` of `profile_common`, 1 when a profile written before it has none in the engine's evaluator) and in the bundle's `weapon_rules`, with the skill of a bow. `roles.ranged_template` = the Melee template with the dexterity weight doubled (0.8 to 1.6) and the armor class weight halved (1.5 to 0.75), the rest as Melee (HP 1.3, MANA 0.5, ATK 3, HASTE 3, END 0.3, SV 0.2, CHA 0.05): a Ranger who shoots is hit less and lives on dexterity, attack and haste; its damage weight is the rule `weapons.damage_weight` times its ATK weight, as Melee's is. Nothing else in the objective changed. **Other changes this needed.** The effects layer reads the Ranged role as a melee role (`ROLE_GROUPS`, the melee column of the click table, the templates it reads gain the Ranged one), so the exalt file of a Ranger trio has the role; the fixture plan draws the lattice and the filler from the roles a trio had (so no other scenario moved) and names the bow scenarios as recipes (the profile of a matrix case of the bow setup is made given the solve pool, a bow being named by a pool alone), the brute-force family has bow instances (every twentieth, from the seventh), the schema of the fixtures lists the new names; the browser engine has the setup in the evaluator, the builder, the pruning, the solve and the value of a shared kit (a kit with a bow in the range place is worth the larger of what its hands make of it and what its bow does, with that setup when the bow's is larger); the page has the role, the setup (only on the trios whose shards have them), the range line and the note of the bow setup (only on those trios). The site reader holds the bundle to the item table for the bow setup as it does for the shield setup (`data._check_bow_setups`). **The page and its budget.** The controls are the role radio of Ranged and the setup radio of Bow (the pages of the trios whose shards have them, and no other), a few bytes in each role's `data-setups`; the range line and the note of the bow setup are made by the page code (`trio.js`, `paintBow`) the first time the setup is shown and are baked into the markup only for a trio whose first answer is the bow's, so a trio page pays for the controls and for nothing else. What the controls cost in the largest page was paid for by leaving out line breaks that no browser draws: the template no longer writes one between the cells of the rows of Wear this, of the totals and of the runner-ups, between the options of a list or between the items of a list, and `build.tighten_main` takes out of the `<main>` of a trio page the ones that sit next to the tag of a block, a list item, a table cell, an option or a summary and the ones before the closing tag of a block unless a span ends there (a break between two inline tags, after the end of a folded block and in the head, the scripts and the footer stays: `test_site_tighten.py`). The controls cost 580 bytes in the page of a trio with a Ranger (the role radio 266, the setup radio 261, the Bow line of the other setups 32 and 7 bytes in each of three attributes), some 310 in the page of a trio with a bow class and no Ranger and nothing in one with none; the breaks left out are 587 bytes of the page of the largest trio. **Real builds of the 15 races of the largest trio** (Ranger / Monk / Shaman; the precompute run in full into a scratch folder with the code of 08c0500 and with this branch's, then the site built from each, indexable and `--noindex`, without the Exalt section, which a release build adds and which is 472 bytes in both): 66,860 bytes (indexable; 66,899 with `--noindex`) before, 66,853 (66,892) after, 7 bytes less (67,061 with the template's changes alone, before `tighten_main`). The release's results (data-2026-10-27) built with the code of 08c0500 make that page 67,338 bytes (246 of the 67,584 that the limit allows are spare), so a release build with this branch makes it 67,331 (253 spare); the next largest page, also a trio with a Ranger, is 349 bytes below it and moves by the same few bytes. Other trios, built the same way but in a world of two races (the Ogre and the Troll; the pages have two race options where a release's have fifteen, so they save fewer line breaks), indexable, with the code of 08c0500 and with this branch's: Monk / Beastlord / Berserker, which has no bow class, 61,514 and 60,966 bytes (548 less, the same page on the screen, 60 scenarios solved to the same rows); Warrior / Cleric / Berserker, which has a bow class and no Ranger, 63,111 and 62,897 (214 less; its grid is 252 scenarios where it was 216); Ranger / Monk / Shaman, 63,285 and 63,317 (32 more, the one case that is not smaller, for the page has two races to save line breaks on, not fifteen). The limit (`build.LIMITS["trio_html"]`, 66 KB) is as it was. **Evidence and the three real trios.** Only the shipped catalog (the wiki's pages) says what a bow is; its 49 archery items give 16 bows in the wiki pool (the classes Warrior, Paladin, Ranger, Shadow Knight and Rogue; 14 for a trio of a given set of those), the rest hidden or ammunition. Three real trios were solved with the new code (the wiki pool, own buffs, every race, every role and setup, +0, +4 and +10; into a scratch folder, not kept): Warrior / Ranger / Berserker (540 scenarios in 300 s on two processes), Ranger / Shaman / Magician (1,230 s) and Cleric / Ranger / Wizard (1,303 s). In all three the Ranged role at +4 and at +10 wears the same pieces for each of the 15 races: Windstriker in the range place, Blood's Unity in the main hand and Bladestopper in the off hand (the hands are stat sticks there; the best race is the Ogre, at +4 and +10 19,835 and 23,665 for the first trio, 22,088 and 26,039 for the second, 20,645 and 24,623 for the third). The three best bows by the measure are the same three at both tiers, with 14 bows in each pool: Windstriker (63 damage in 60 delay at +4, 90 at +10: 1.55 and 2.0 a delay unit before the haste), Distressed Mithril Shortbow (35 in 40 and 50 in 40: 1.375 and 1.75) and Bow of the Underfoot (46 in 60 and 66 in 60: 1.267 and 1.6). The range place held a bow in every one of the 450 solved scenarios of the setup, and no kit wore a second bow in an Any place. The bow setup is the best setup of Balanced, Tank or Melee in none of their 405 views (race, role and tier; dual wield is, or the stat stick for Caster: for the Ogre at +4 the best setup of Balanced is dual wield, 19,993 against 19,595 for the bow, in the first trio). Nothing else moved: the 24 chunks of the old setups of the first trio (two races) and all the chunks, the runner-ups included, of three trios with no class that can use a bow (Cleric / Druid / Shaman, Wizard / Magician / Enchanter, Monk / Beastlord / Berserker; two races, +0 +4 +10, wiki pool, own buffs, 9 chunks and 3 runner-up chunks each) are row for row the ones the code of 08c0500 makes, to the last digit. **What a full rebuild needs.** Of the 560 trios, 395 have a class that may equip a bow (the other 165 have no new scenario) and 105 have a Ranger (and so a role more). The grid of the scope of a release (+0, +4 and +10, every tier for the milestone trio) goes from about 677,100 scenarios in 45,140 chunks to 791,040 in 52,736: 113,940 scenarios in 7,596 chunks are new (the bow setup in Balanced, Tank and Melee, and the Ranged role), and every trio with a bow class has them. A new scenario costs what an old one does in its trio (0.34 against 0.32 s of solve and build for a Warrior / Ranger / Berserker, 3.5 against 4.4 s for the two trios with a Shaman or a Wizard whose pools mix WIS and INT), so the new scenarios are some 11 CPU hours at the cheaper rate, 136 at the dearer and about 80 at the rate of the three trios solved together, plus the runner-up chunks of the Ranged role (three a Ranger trio). The code hash of the chunks (`grid._code_hash`: `model/`, `solver/`, `precompute/grid.py`) and the hash of `rules/rules.json` are in the fingerprint of every chunk and both changed, so as the release build stands **every chunk of every trio is made again**, the 677,100 that have the answers they had included (the whole grid is about seven times the new work, some 550 CPU hours at the central rate), unless the fingerprints of the unchanged chunks are carried over by a tool that this task did not write. The hash of the writers (`assemble.WRITER_FILES`: `export/browser.py`, `precompute/__init__.py`, `effects/judgment.py` and `effects/layer.py` changed) changes too, which writes the files of a finished trio again from its chunks and solves nothing. The default views (the best race and its margin, the best setup of each role) are read again for every trio with a bow class, since a bow may now be the best setup; none was, in the trios solved. The bundle of every trio gains `ranged_factor` in `profile_common` and the bow factor and skill in `weapon_rules`, and `items.json` is unchanged; the site build needs no new input. **Tests** (new, shown failing first: `test_ranged.py` had 27 failures before the model knew the setup; `tests-js/ranged.test.mjs` could not read a bundle with no `ranged_factor`; `tests-js/ranged-page.test.mjs` had no page to read): `test_ranged.py` (rules, vocabulary, which setups and roles are offered, the measure of a bow, the evaluator, the profile, the builder and the solver, the pruning, the canonical placement of a spare bow, the exact agreement with the brute force), `test_ranged_data.py` (the reader's check of the bundle against the item table), `test_ranged_site.py` (a real run of the precompute on an invented catalog with bows, for a trio with a Ranger and a trio with no bow class, its shards, its bundle, the page it builds, and the contract files of `fixtures/site/contract-ranged/` that the page code is tested against), `tests-js/ranged.test.mjs` (the engine), `tests-js/ranged-page.test.mjs` (the controls in jsdom: the role and the setup offered, chosen, written to the address and read back from it), `tests-e2e/ranged.spec.mjs` (the page of a trio with no bow has none of it, and a link that asks for it means nothing there), `test_site_tighten.py` (what the pass over the line breaks of a trio page takes out and what it leaves; written with the function, as a refinement of the payment, not before it); the parity fixtures have the setup in the matrix (four recipes and two cases of the sample), the brute-force family (every twentieth instance, from the seventh, half of them truthful) and the term cases of the evaluator. Existing tests edited (each only to say what the new names add; none weakened, skipped or deleted): `test_weapons.py` (the main hand of the bow setup takes anything, as the stick's does), `test_roles.py` (the roles of a Ranger trio end with Ranged, Balanced does not average it, its damage weight is its template's), `test_rules.py` (the two new ids and sources), `test_bench.py` (the same roles), `test_export_browser.py` (the wiki profile is made given the wiki pool; a pool may bring the bow setup that the no-pool setups lack), `test_export_fixtures.py` (a bow scenario is checked against the wiki pool; a profile made with no pool has no bow setup), `test_export_evaluate_terms.py` and `tests-js/engine-evaluate.test.mjs` (the setups of the cases), `tests-js/engine-profile.test.mjs` (28 fields, one more exact text), `pipeline/tests/solver_helpers.py` (the helpers know the range place and the setup; one more scope, a trio with a Ranger, among `PICKS`), `pipeline/tests/engine_fixture.py` (its one invented archery item is a thrown weapon now, so that the fixture trio has no bow class and its shards and runner-ups are as they were) and the committed files the tests read, written again by their own generators and no assertion touched: the parity fixtures and the evaluator's term file (`ranged_factor` in every profile, the bow cases), `fixtures/site/results*` (the engine data of the site fixture) `fixtures/effects/plan-parity.json` (the effects layer has the Ranged role, so the seeded cases of the plan fixture moved, and 4 lists and 8 cases of its sample are of that role; the browser's matcher reads the lists it is given and needed no change), and the contract pages `fixtures/site/contract/trio-main.html` and `contract-exalt/trio-main.html` (they differ from before only by the line breaks left out).

Now the plain instances, scenarios and kits are drawn exactly as at 08c0500 (the seeds, the cover of the strata, the roles the lattice and the filler draw from, the roles and setups the evaluator and the weight cases draw from are the old ones) and the bow cases come after them: brute instances 1000 and up (one for every twenty plain ones, every second a truthful one), the four matrix scenarios of `fixture_plan.bow_plan` numbered after the whole plan (097 to 100 in the sample), and one evaluator kit of the bow setup for every twenty, most of them with a bow in the range place, drawn from a stream of their own. The whole sample of 08c0500 (40 brute, 11 matrix, 6 pmb36, 7 upgrade, 150 kits, 150 items, the profiles) is in the new one with the same values (compared file by file: every value equal but for the key `ranged_factor` of a profile, which every profile has now, and the bow setup in the `setups` of a profile made given its pool, and the new bundle constant `weapon_rules.bow_skill`); the manifest counts are the old ones and more (brute 40 to 42, matrix 11 to 15, evaluate 150 to 157, matrix coverage 36 to 40 tags) and no tag of any family has a lower count (evaluate: haste-capped 10 to 10, dual wield 32 to 32, mixed tiers 36 to 37, END over 201 61 to 61, shield worn 44 to 45). **The profile of a scenario is the one the pool makes.** A matrix, pmb36 or upgrade scenario was made with no pool to read (a shield assumed, no bow) and test_export_fixtures held it equal to `assemble` of the bundle at its evidence level only because the test pools had nothing to tell them apart; it is made given the solve pool of its evidence level now, which is what `assemble` gives at that level, so that equality holds, unmodified, for every scenario (the loosening that left the bow setup out of both sides is gone). **The invented catalog's archery item is a bow again** (its class list has the Paladin), so the contract fixture of the Paladin trio (`fixtures/site/results-solved`, a real run of the precompute on the invented catalog) has the Bow setup: its 80 rows of the four old setups are the rows of 08c0500 row for row, and 32 rows of the bow setup are added, and the engine's best-kit parity holds all 112 to the browser's solve. **Archery.** The client's skill caps have the skill (`derived/client/skillcaps.json`, skill 7: a cap for Warrior, Paladin, Ranger, Shadow Knight and Rogue at 50, 55 and 60, for no other class, which are the classes of the catalog's bows), so the bow setup is gated as dual wield and the shield setup are: a class of the trio must have a cap above 0 at the level and be in the class list of a bow of the pool (`weapons.archers`, `bow_available(tables, level)`). **The Ranged role is not offered for a pool with no bow.** It plays the bow and nothing else, so for a pool with no bow for the trio it has no setup (the stat stick it used to fall back to is gone): `setups_for` gives `[]`, `roles_for(trio, bow=False)` leaves it out, a profile of it for such a pool is refused, and with no pool to read the bow is assumed, as a shield is. The roles of a trio are the roles of the widest pool (the wiki one, `grid.scenarios`, `browser.bundle`), and a level whose own pool has no bow (the strict one, in the public catalog) has the role with no setup and no rows: the bundle lists `[]` for it at that level, a shard lists the role in `setups` with `[]` (the reader counts it as the shard's role, `Shard.unplayed`, and the bundle must say it has no setup there), the browser engine's reader accepts an empty list at one level (and refuses one at every level) and refuses a profile of it there, and the check of the engine's data leaves such a profile out. **The range place is filled in the bow setup.** The builder has a row, `range.bow` (sum of the range columns at least 1), the exporter's space of kits and the test helpers' hold a bow in the range place of every kit, the browser's builder has the same row, and a pool with no bow for the place is a `ValueError` in all of them; a bow is worn though damage is worth nothing (custom ATK 0), as the tests of both sides say. **`roles.ranged_template` is a rule of differences** (value `{DEX: 2, AC: 0.5}`, the factors of the dexterity and armor class weights) applied to whatever `roles.templates.melee` is when the weights are made (`roles.weights_for`, the effects layer), so a later change to Melee carries over; the numbers it gives today are the ones the first round froze (1.6 and 0.75), so no solve changed; the notes say it is a judgment and name no outside number. **Notes that cite the rule.** For the Ranged role the three notes that cite the role weights (under the role, under the best race, under the runner-ups) name `roles.ranged_template` beside `roles.templates` (the template is the Melee one with the factors), with the seal of the templates (a test holds the two seals equal): the page code makes the link when the role is chosen or asked for, from `data-rule` and `data-rule-title` on the radio of the role (79 bytes in the page of a trio with a Ranger, none in another), so the other roles are as they were. **The tightened page.** `test_site_tighten_css.py` keeps what `build.tighten_main` was given, finds every element next to a line break the pass leaves out, and reads the built stylesheet for a rule that makes such an element inline (the subject compound of a rule is matched, its ancestors are not: it can only be too careful); inside an inline-block, flex or grid container the white space at its start and end is not drawn, after its closing tag it is, and an element that is `inline` loses nothing either way. It passes on the real build (its one find, `.seg-items`, an inline-flex container, draws no white space inside) and fails on a rule that makes a left-out element inline. **Budget.** Real builds of the 15 races of the largest trio (Ranger / Monk / Shaman) with this branch: 66,932 bytes indexable (66,971 with `--noindex`) against 66,860 (66,899) at 08c0500, 72 more (the rule on the radio, 79 bytes, less the 7 that the first round saved); a release build is about 67,410 of the 67,584 the limit allows, 174 spare (246 at 08c0500). **Stopped on: the shared kit.** The first round valued a shared kit with a bow in the range place under both its hands' setup and the bow setup and kept the larger, which no Python reference does and which spent a second search of the buffs outside the budget of calls (a test of the budget failed on it once the contract fixture's kit wore a bow). A link lists the items of a kit with their places and tiers and nothing else (`input/kitcode.js`, "not what the visitor owns, not a value, not a word"), so it cannot say that the kit was made for the bow setup, and a kit is valued under the setup its hands are (as at 08c0500); I removed the larger-of-two valuation and did not change the link: a shared kit made for the bow setup is valued with its bow unscored. Giving the link an optional setup is a decision for you (the format, the address, the reader of it and the kit-values fixture). **Tests.** New (shown failing first): `pipeline/tests/test_ranged_round2.py` (15 failed before the code; the Archery gate, the roles of a pool, the forced bow and its refusal, the space of kits, the rule of differences and its carrying over, the shield), `tests-js/ranged-round2.test.mjs` (the forced bow failed with the row left out), the jsdom tests of the cited rule (`tests-js/ranged-page.test.mjs`), `test_ranged_site.py` (a Ranger trio whose pool has no bow; the rule on the radio; the strict level's role with no setup), `test_site_tighten_css.py`. the equality of the stored profile and `assemble` is as it was), `test_export_browser.py` (the profile of a role the pool does not offer at a level is not asked for), `tests-js/engine-profile.test.mjs` (an empty list of setups at one evidence level is read and one at every level refused), `tests-js/best-kit-parity-real.test.mjs` (112 views in 56 chunks where there were 80 in 40: the four old setups' and the bow setup's), `pipeline/tests/solver_helpers.py` (the space of kits fills the range place in the bow setup), `test_ranged.py` and `test_roles.py` (the Ranged template as the Melee one with the factors, the Archery skill in the invented skill table, the no-pool and no-bow cases of the role), and the committed files that their own generators write again (`fixtures/parity`, `fixtures/site/results*`, `fixtures/site/contract-ranged`). **The old setups are unchanged, shown directly.** The chunks of a run of the code of 08c0500 against a run of this branch's, chunk by chunk and row by row (the value, the kit, the buffs, the haste, the totals, and the pool and pruning counts of the chunk), for three trios with a bow class (Warrior / Cleric / Berserker, Paladin / Monk / Berserker and Warrior / Ranger / Berserker; and for three trios with no bow class (Monk / Beastlord / Berserker, Cleric / Druid / Shaman, Wizard / Magician / Enchanter; all 15 races, the whole grid of the scope): 30 chunks (450 rows) each are identical, none is new, and the runner-up chunks are identical. Nothing is claimed to be byte for byte: for a trio with no bow class the solved numbers are the old ones, and what changes is the bundle (`ranged_factor` in every profile, `weapon_rules.bow_skill`), the page (the line breaks left out, so it is some 580 bytes smaller and is drawn the same), and the hashes (the code of the chunks, the rules file and the writers), so that a release build makes every chunk again.
- 2026-11-18 The Bow setup and the Ranged role, round 3 (branch ranged-role). **A bow is scored on its own damage per delay.** The first round scored a bow with the measure of a melee main hand, `(damage + bonus) / delay`, the bonus being the one the page lists, else half the delay (the pattern the melee weapons follow): Windstriker, 45 damage in 60 delay, scored (45 + 30) / 60. That was so, and it was a main hand's rule given to a bow: no bow of the catalog lists a damage bonus (none of its 38 bows lists one), no rule has a public or measured source that says archery gets the bonus, and the client data says nothing of it. So the bow in the range place of the Bow setup is scored `damage / delay x (1 + haste / 100) x weapons.ranged_factor` (`weapons.damage_index_exact`, `formulas.weapon_index`, the builder's columns through them, `evaluate.js` and `model.js` of the browser engine), with no bonus whether the page lists one or not, and no arrow damage. The note of `weapons.ranged_factor` says so in plain words and still calls it a judgment: what is counted (the bow's damage over its delay, at the haste, times the factor), what is not (the damage bonus of a main hand, arrow damage, anything else about shooting). What it changes: the same constant (`0.5 x (1 + haste/100) x weight`) is no longer added to a bow's score, so the value of every Bow-setup scenario falls by about 0.5 x the damage weight x the haste factor (the Ranged role of the three solved trios, Ogre, +4: 19,835 to 18,530, 22,088 to 20,513, 20,645 to 19,385; +10: 23,665 to 22,306, 26,039 to 24,464, 24,623 to 23,309), the order of the bows is the order of their damage per delay as before (the bonus was the same half delay in every bow's index, a constant before the haste factor), and the value of the other setups does not change, so the Bow setup is further from the best setup of Balanced, Tank and Melee than it was. **No pick moved.** In the three real trios (Warrior / Ranger / Berserker, Ranger / Shaman / Magician, Cleric / Ranger / Wizard; the Ranged role, every race, +4 and +10, wiki pool, own buffs) the range place, the main hand and the off hand are the same in every one of the 90 scenarios: Windstriker, Blood's Unity and Bladestopper. The three best bows by the measure are the same three, with other numbers: Windstriker 1.05, Distressed Mithril Shortbow 0.875 and Bow of the Underfoot 0.767 at +4 (they were 1.55, 1.375 and 1.267), 1.5, 1.25 and 1.1 at +10 (2.0, 1.75, 1.6). The old setups are untouched (the parity sample's 08c0500 cases and the contract fixture's 80 rows of the four old setups are the same as before), and the files that hold bow values are written again: `fixtures/evaluate-terms.json`, the bow cases of `fixtures/parity`, the 32 bow rows of `fixtures/site/results-solved` and the contract fixture of the Ranger trio. **The kit link has an optional setup marker.** Item 6 of the second round was stopped on because a link lists items and tiers only. It now can say, once, that the kit was made for the Bow setup: a link is version 1 (the text it always was, for every kit with no marker, read to the kit it always was) or version 2, which is version 1's fields and then one number, the code of the setup (`SETUP_CODES`, 1 is `ranged`; the table is part of the format and only grows at the end). Only a kit made for the Bow setup is written as version 2: Equip now's Share when the weapon setup shown was the Bow's, and the Share of a shared kit that has the marker. A reader that gets a code it does not know (0, or past the table) reads the kit with no marker, so a link of a later version still opens; the engine accepts a marker (`madeFor` of `valueOfKit`: `setup` is still refused) only for `ranged`, only if the bundle lists the Bow setup for some role at the evidence level (the trio offers it) and only if the kit has a bow in its range place, and ignores it otherwise, so a forged marker cannot change a kit's items or give a trio a setup it lacks, only choose between the setup of the hands and the Bow's for a kit that wears a bow; an accepted marker gives `setup: ranged` and `named: true` and the line says "as the link says" where it said "read from the kit's hands" (the answer of a kit with no accepted marker has the keys it had). The value of a Bow kit from a link is the value of its precomputed row, to the two decimals the shard keeps (all 8 rows of the contract fixture). **Budgets.** The evaluator of a shared kit (`kitvalue`) was 78,275 bytes raw and 26,421 gzipped (76.4 and 25.8 KB), is 78,629 and 26,554 (76.8 and 25.9 KB), of 80 KB and 28 KB; the largest trio page is as it was (66,932 bytes indexable in a real build of the 15 races of the Ranger / Monk / Shaman trio, about 67,410 in a release build, 174 spare), for round 3 writes no markup; the first load of the page script is 16.7 KB gzipped of 30. **Tests** (new, shown failing first): `test_ranged_round3.py` (the measure, the listed bonus, the factor, the two definitions, the evaluator, the builder, the ranking, the note, the catalog's bows), `tests-js/ranged-round3.test.mjs`, `tests-js/kitcode-setup.test.mjs` (an old link is written and read as it was, a Bow link is version 2 and round-trips, a code it does not know is read as none, a setup that is not in the table is refused), `tests-js/kitlink-setup.test.mjs` (a Bow link's value is its row's, an old link's is as it was, a marker the trio does not offer or a kit with no bow is ignored), and the page tests of both Share buttons and of the shared-kit panel. Existing tests edited (none weakened): `tests-js/kitcode.test.mjs` (version 2 is no longer one of the versions that are refused), `test_ranged.py` and `tests-js/ranged.test.mjs` (the first round's bow measure, mine), and the committed files their generators write again.
- 2026-11-18 The Bow setup and the Ranged role, round 4 (branch ranged-role; after the cross-family review of round 3). **One policy for a pool that is not there.** With no pool to read, a shield is assumed (the design grid of a trio has one) and a bow is not, for every role: no role has `ranged` among its setups with no pool, and the Ranged role, which is nothing but the bow setup, has none either, so it is not offered with no pool (`weapons.setups_for` gives `[]` for it, `roles_for(trio)` leaves it out, `make_profile` refuses it, a grid made with no pool has no scenario of it, and a bundle gives its profile only at an evidence level: `roles.POOL_ROLES`, `browser.assemble`, `assembleProfile`). Round 3 had the Ranged role say `ranged` with no pool while the other roles did not, and skip the Archery skill gate; the other choice, assuming the bow for every role as the shield is, was tried and fails 14 tests of 08c0500 (the setups of every Warrior, Paladin, Ranger, Shadow Knight and Rogue trio with no pool), so it was left. `roles_for(trio, bow=None)` is what a caller that asks about the roles of a trio before any pool is in question passes (the weights of a role, the effects layer, the order of the chunks of a run, the guess of the weight of a trio in a release); the plain `roles_for(trio)` is the roles of a trio with no pool, which are the roles it had, so the old tests of `roles_for` stand as they were, and the lattice and the filler of the fixture plan and the shapes of the benchmark draw from the old roles. The pool may be any iterable (`setups_for` reads it once into a list; it was read twice, for the bow and for the shield). **The page and the code.** The note of the Bow setup says what is counted (the bow's own damage per delay, no main-hand bonus, no arrow damage); the other-setups line of the Bow shows the bow and the hands as the others show theirs (`Bow: Windstriker with Sword + Shield (-730)`: the hands are stat sticks, and a shield there counts its armor class); the note of the page code follows the setup even when the weapon list is not there; a kit link's setup marker is forwarded and valued only when it is the Bow's (as the Upgrade answer already did). Each has a weight template (`roles.templates`, seal model: Tank HP 1, AC 12, MANA 0.15, ATK 2.67, DEX 0.1, HASTE 1.5, END 0.5, SV 0.4, CHA 0.05; Shield caster AC 12, MANA 0.3, ATK 0.3, SV 1.5, no haste; Melee damage HP 1.3, AC 1.5, ATK 4, DEX 0.8, HASTE 3; Caster tank AC 9, MANA 1, SV 0.8; Caster MANA 4, AC 1.2, SV 0.8; the Archer is the Melee template with dexterity doubled and armor class halved, `roles.ranged_template`) and a factor on the weapon's damage (`roles.weapon_factor`: Tank 0.5, Melee damage and Archer 1, the three others 0), with `weapons.damage_weight` 450 (it was 600 when Melee damage weighed attack 3 and now weighs 4: the weapon weight of Melee damage stays 1800). - 2026-11-20 A kit is chosen on gear haste alone (`haste.assume_spell`, false, seal model, a judgment: a trio may or may not have a hasting class awake, so a worn haste item is worth its full value up to the cap). With the rule false the haste of a spell is left out of the buffs a kit is chosen with: the spell haste of a profile is the Monk's flat haste alone. The rule true gives the profile exactly what it gave before. **The reader's toggle.** The bundle of a trio keeps, beside each of its four buff sets, a twin that keeps the haste of the spells (`own+haste`, `group+haste`, `group+enchanter+haste`, `raid+haste`, with `buff_table_haste` for the buffs that carry haste: one spell id has two readings, Augmentation among them), and the trio page has a box "I have a haste spell" in its Buffs assumption (off by default; the address says `haste=1` only when it is on, and anything else is off). A kit with the box on is solved in the browser with the twin of the buff set chosen; the shards, which are made without a haste spell, are not offered for it. The parity of the toggle: the twins are, row for row, the sets that a bundle made with the rule true holds (`test_export_browser`), the engine fixtures hold the best kit, the Equip now kits and the kit values under the twins to Python's, and the profile fixtures list every race, build and twin. No rule changed by the toggle.
- 2026-11-20 Weapon procs count in the weapon term, and the Lore rule (branch builds; `model/procs.py`, `solver/model.py`, `solver/prune.py`, `model/formulas.py`, `static/engine/procs.js`, `evaluate.js`, `model.js`, `prune.js`; the rules of what a proc is worth are `effects.proc_*`). **What.** A hand that swings adds `DMG x value / 600` for the proc it counts, with no haste factor (a proc is paced by the minute, the swing by the haste), the off hand by the dual-wield factor; one proc a hand; below the socket tier (+4) a weapon counts its own best proc only, at or above it the best of its own and the procs it can host from a donor of its slot and classes. **One physical copy supplies one hand** (a Lore item is one copy, any other item two). **The Lore rule (new in this stage): the copy a kit wears is the copy it wears.** A Lore item that the kit wears anywhere (in a hand, an Any place, the range place or any other place) is not a donor for a hand it is not in; its own proc still counts in the hand that wears it. It is a conservative rule (it was open whether a Lore donor worn in a third place can also be moved): the evaluator is given the Lore items of the kit, the program has a binary for each way a proc can be counted in a hand when a hosted proc has a Lore source (rows `proc.host`, `proc.one`, `proc.worn`, `proc.copies`), and with no Lore source the proc is a constant on the column as before; the pruning never drops a Lore donor for another item and a Lore donor drops none. **Parity.** The bundle carries the table (`procs`), the browser's port reads it, and the brute-force family of the parity fixtures has instances with a table (the program, the pruned pool and the kit value are held to the brute force and to Python in the browser's tests); the independent enumeration of the copies in `test_procs_milp` holds the Python program to the rule. No value of any rule changed; the kits of a trio with procs change where a Lore donor was worn twice over.

The Role choice is labelled Build, one line of hint for each (`ROLE_HINTS`). **Old links keep working:** `role=balanced`, a build the trio is not offered or a value that is no build opens the opening build with no error; `role=tank`, `melee`, `caster` and `ranged` keep their ids; a weapon setup a build no longer has opens the build's best setup; kit links and the `w` and `p` parameters are read as they were (each is tested). Where the data lists two races or more as close to the best (`close_races` of the defaults file, the rule above) the line of the best race says "Races for this trio, close: A, B, C (within 1%)" where it named a best race, the dropdown marks each close race (close for this trio), and the cards of "What each race gets you" say "close to X"; the old band of 0.5 percent that the page judged by itself is removed (the page judges nothing of its own). **Your priorities** has one button on the Tank and the Shield caster, "Resists first": resists Top, armor class Low, the rest Normal; it sets the selects like any choice (so `p` says it), and Reset clears it. The largest trio page is limited to 67,584 bytes in the indexable build (not raised), the first-load script to 30 KB gzip and the evaluator to 81,920 bytes raw and 28,672 gzip; the figures before and after this stage are in the report of the branch.
